### [1] [Surgical Video Generation From Diffusion to World Models: A Survey](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26214)
*Fuxiang Huang,Chenxu Zhang,Liang Han,Lei Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This survey organizes the 2024-2026 literature on surgical video generation into three categories (unconditional, conditional, and world modeling generation), revealing a shift from frame synthesis to causal dynamics modeling, while identifying key bottlenecks in generalization, physical realism, controllability, and interpretability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Surgical video data is constrained by privacy, cost, and class imbalance, creating a critical need for synthetic data generation to support surgical simulation, training, and robotic policy learning. The field lacks a conceptual framework to organize rapid developments.

Method: Systematic survey of 2024-2026 literature, categorizing methods into three generations types, examining the gap between pixel-level fidelity and clinical plausibility, and summarizing experimental results on public datasets.

Result: The field has shifted from synthesizing visually plausible frames to modeling causal dynamics of surgical scenes. Key bottlenecks identified: generalization, physical realism, controllability, and interpretability. Quantitative reference provided through representative method comparisons.

Conclusion: The survey offers a structured overview of current state and open challenges for researchers at the intersection of intelligent perception, multi-modal fusion, generative AI, and surgical data science.

Abstract: Surgical video data provides the primary training resource for models of intraoperative perception, surgical workflow understanding, and robotic decision-making. However, clinical data acquisition remains constrained by privacy, cost, and class imbalance. Surgical video generation has emerged as a transformative approach to addressing data scarcity and as a foundation for surgical simulation, training, and robotic policy learning. The field has developed rapidly without a clear conceptual framework. This survey organizes the 2024-2026 literature into three categories: unconditional generation, conditional generation, and world modeling generation, revealing a fundamental shift in how the task is defined from synthesizing visually plausible frames to modeling the causal dynamics of surgical scenes. We examine the persistent gap between pixel-level fidelity and clinical plausibility, and identify generalization, physical realism, controllability, and interpretability as bottlenecks. We further summarize experimental results of representative methods on public datasets to provide a quantitative reference for the field. This survey provides a structured overview of the current state and open challenges, offering a reference for researchers working at the intersection of intelligent perception, multi-modal fusion, generative AI, and surgical data science.

</details>


### [2] [Procedura: Agentic 3D Modeling with Procedural Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26238)
*Youtian Lin,Yikang Yang,Zhanpeng Hu,Mengqi Zhou,Feihu Zhang,Xun Cao,Jiaheng Liu,Yao Yao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Procedura is a novel 3D modeling agent that generates objects as editable procedural programs with typed, machine-checkable mates from text prompts, using LLM to write parametric assemblies with part decomposition and sharp edges.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Native 3D generators from single images produce soft, smooth meshes lacking sharp edges, part decomposition, and editability - they cannot represent machined objects with precise geometric features or provideEditable parameters for users to modify.

Method: Procedura uses an LLM to write objects as procedural assemblies: (1) plans object as assembly graph from text prompt, (2) writes program part-by-part solving placements from mated frames, (3) validates through compile, mate, and connectivity checks, (4) refines via decoupled vision critic diagnosing and fixing issues incrementally, (5) carries per-part materials and simulator-validated articulation.

Result: Outperforms state-of-the-art native 3D generators and all prior 3D-code agents on P3D-Bench and MechBench-36 (hard-surface benchmark) under assembly judge evaluation. Produces sharpest edges of any evaluated method and is the only approach delivering editable, part-structured program output.

Conclusion: The '3D as code' paradigm successfully leverages LLM coding abilities for 3D modeling, creating a new frontier where generated 3D content is parametric, editable, physically valid, and structurally decomposed - addressing fundamental limitations of native mesh generation.

Abstract: Native 3D generators now recover impressive mesh geometry from a single image. However, a dense mesh stays soft where a machined object should be sharp, it carries no part decomposition, and it exposes no parameter a user could edit. To address this, we explore the paradigm of 3D shape as code, leveraging and scaling the coding ability of an LLM for 3D modeling. We introduce Procedura, a novel 3D modeling agent framework that writes an object as a procedural assembly, a parametric program whose named parts are joined by typed, machine-checkable mates. From a text prompt, the agent plans the object as an assembly graph and writes the program part by part, solving each placement from the mated frames rather than guessing it, and admitting a part only once compile, mate, and connectivity checks pass. A decoupled vision critic then refines the assembly one diagnosed fix at a time. Moreover, the same graph carries per-part materials and a simulator-validated articulation. We evaluate on P3D-Bench under its assembly judge, and with the same judge on MechBench-36, our hard-surface benchmark. On both, Procedura outperforms state-of-the-art native 3D generators and every prior 3D-code agent on judged quality, produces the sharpest edges of any method we evaluate, and is the only one whose output is an editable, part-structured program.

</details>


### [3] [Modality Maturity Index: A benchmark for assessing multimodal capabilities of omni models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26317)
*Rohit Patel,Dieuwke Hupkes,Sloan Strader*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes the Modality Maturity Index (MMI), a benchmark for evaluating multimodal large language models across five modalities (text, image, audio, video, document) with up to three input/output modalities. Testing five frontier models reveals low Modality Presence Scores (15.6-34.9%), indicating significant gaps in multimodal generation capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current evaluation frameworks focus almost exclusively on bimodal understanding (text plus one modality), despite frontier language models being marketed as omni-systems capable of perceiving and responding across multiple modalities. There is a need for comprehensive evaluation of true multimodal capabilities.

Method: MMI consists of 893 carefully crafted questions requiring models to demonstrate understanding of multiple input modalities and generate responses incorporating various output formats. Each prompt includes human-authored rubric criteria for expected output modalities. The study tested five frontier multimodal models and conducted a separate experiment comparing LLM judge performance against human annotators using custom generation tools.

Result: Modality Presence Scores (MPS) ranged from 15.6 (Claude Opus 4.6) to 34.9 (GPT-5.4), indicating low availability of returned modalities. An LLM judge applying rubrics agreed with rubric-blind human annotators on 70.8% of judgments, demonstrating moderate viability for automated evaluation.

Conclusion: Current frontier multimodal models show significant limitations in generating multiple modalities. Given these low scores, MPS is reported as the main result pending model improvements. The LLM-based judging approach shows promise but requires further refinement, with 70.8% human-LLM agreement suggesting it's a viable but imperfect evaluation method.

Abstract: Frontier language models are increasingly marketed as omni systems that can perceive and respond across modalities. Existing evaluation frameworks, however, focus almost exclusively on bimodal understanding, typically text plus one other modality. We propose the Modality Maturity Index (MMI), a benchmark designed to evaluate the multimodal capabilities of large language models across five modalities (text, image, audio, video and document) and combinations of up to three modalities in both inputs and outputs. MMI consists of 893 questions, each carefully crafted to require the model to demonstrate its understanding of multiple input modalities and to generate responses that incorporate various output formats. The questions are designed to be self-contained, with clear expectations for the correct modality or mix of modalities required for an accurate response. Every MMI prompt carries human-authored rubric criteria for each output modality expected in the response; a model's MMI Value expresses the average of the per-modality scores for each prompt. Because low scores can reflect either failure to generate a modality (lack of presence) or failure to generate correct content, we introduce also a supplementary Modality Presence Score (MPS), a per-prompt F1 over the expected output modalities. Applying MMI to five frontier multimodal models, we find that the MPS ranges from only 15.6 (Claude Opus 4.6) to 34.9 (GPT-5.4). Given the low availability of returned modalities to even grade, we report MPS as our main result pending model improvements. To assess the viability of judging output correctness with LLM judges and rubrics, we run a separate experiment with custom generation tools. On the assets that generates, we find that an LLM judge applying the rubrics agrees with rubric-blind human annotators (who score the outputs directly and never see the criteria) on 70.8% of judgments.

</details>


### [4] [Finding the Right Evidence: Factor-Guided Coarse-to-Fine Reasoning for Long Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26355)
*Baixuan Xu,Yinyui Xu,Tianshi Zheng,Zhaowei Wang,Weiqi Wang,Haochen Shi,Jiayu Liu,Qing Zong,Xiyu Ren,Xinyu Geng,Zhitao He,Yangqiu Song*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PACE (Progressive Acquisition of Critical Evidence) is a two-stage framework for long-video question answering that first indexes clip-level descriptions using question-derived factors, then uses candidate answers to derive contrastive cues for verification. It addresses the bottleneck of option-discriminative evidence rather than topical relevance alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long-video question answering remains challenging because relevant evidence is sparse and question-relevant context often fails to discriminate correct answers from plausible alternatives. Diagnostic analysis reveals that prior agentic systems improve cue retrieval but fail to achieve corresponding gains in answer accuracy, indicating the bottleneck lies in option-discriminative evidence rather than topical relevance.

Method: PACE employs a two-stage factor-guided approach: (1) Indexing stage - generates clip-level descriptions guided by question-derived factors without observing candidate answers; (2) Verification stage - uses candidate answers to derive contrastive cues and queries the index for verification. The method uses Qwen3-VL as the backbone.

Result: On MMR-V with Qwen3-VL backbone, PACE achieves 42.6% accuracy, outperforming direct inference and prior agentic baselines including Deep Video Discovery (DVD). On the diagnostic subset, PACE recovers 66.9% of annotated cues. Consistent gains over DVD are observed on LVBench, Video-MME, EgoSchema, and LongVideoBench.

Conclusion: The gains from PACE are associated with improved evidence recovery rather than stronger answer-side priors alone. Option-aware evidence acquisition transfers beyond MMR-V to multiple long-video benchmarks, demonstrating the importance of focusing on option-discriminative evidence for long-video QA tasks.

Abstract: While LVLMs rapidly improve, long-video question answering still remains challenging: relevant evidence is sparse, and question-relevant context often fails to provide cues that discriminate the correct answer from plausible alternatives. Diagnostic analysis on a manually annotated subset of MMR-V shows that prior agentic systems substantially improve cue retrieval over direct VLM inference yet fail to achieve a corresponding gain in answer accuracy, indicating that the bottleneck lies in option-discriminative evidence rather than topical relevance alone. We propose PACE (Progressive Acquisition of Critical Evidence), a factor-guided framework for long-video evidence acquisition. PACE proceeds in two stages: it first indexes clip-level descriptions guided by question-derived factors without observing the candidate answers; it then uses the candidate answers to derive contrastive cues and queries the index for verification. On MMR-V with the open-source Qwen3-VL backbone, PACE achieves 42.6% accuracy, outperforming direct inference and prior agentic baselines including Deep Video Discovery (DVD). On the same diagnostic subset, PACE recovers 66.9% of the annotated cues, providing empirical evidence that its gains are associated with improved evidence recovery rather than stronger answer-side priors alone. Consistent gains over DVD on LVBench, Video-MME, EgoSchema, and LongVideoBench suggest that option-aware evidence acquisition transfers beyond MMR-V. Code is available at https://github.com/HKUST-KnowComp/PACE.

</details>


### [5] [A Unified Framework for the Mechanics of Information in Convolutional Neural Network Image Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26363)
*Aryan Shukla,Matthew Toews*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a unified mathematical framework linking convolutional neural network (CNN) filter operations to physical laws, specifically drawing an analogy between discrete filter symmetry and the relativistic energy-momentum relation. Symmetric filters preserve image center of mass (like rest energy mc²), while antisymmetric filters induce displacement (like momentum pc). The framework connects to Gaussian scale-space, heat/Schrödinger equations, Friedmann equations, and Morse topology, with demonstrations across scales from molecules to galaxies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to bridge the gap between descriptions of physical space and information space by developing a unified mathematical framework for modeling information propagation through CNNs. They seek to establish connections between discrete filter operations in neural networks and fundamental physical equations.

Method: The paper develops a mathematical correspondence between discrete filter symmetry properties and relativistic physics. The authors analyze how symmetric filter components (preserving center of mass) and antisymmetric components (inducing displacement) behave under repeated convolution, drawing analogies to the relativistic energy-momentum relation E² = (pc)² + (mc²)². They apply this framework to analyze 3D images across multiple scales.

Result: The framework reveals that repeated filtering leads to Gaussian scale-space and emergent scale-invariant features. The constructions share Laplacian-driven structure with the classical heat equation and connect to the Schrödinger equation and Friedmann equations. Emergent Morse topological structures are observed. Demonstrations across physical scales—from organic sugar molecules and silicon crystals to human brains, galaxies, and cosmic microwave background—reveal blob-like, scale-invariant Morse critical points.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a deep mathematical connection between CNN filter operations and fundamental physical equations, suggesting that information propagation in neural networks follows similar mathematical structures to those governing physical phenomena across all scales, from quantum to cosmic.

Abstract: This paper introduces a unified mathematical framework for modeling information propagation through convolutional neural networks (CNNs), with the aim of connecting descriptions of physical space and information space.
  A correspondence is presented linking discrete filter symmetry and the relativistic energy--momentum relation under the widely used nonlinear rectified convolution operation. Specifically, symmetric filter components (e.g. the sum $Σ= [1,1]$) operate analogously to rest energy $mc^2$ in preserving the image centre of mass (e.g. isotropic diffusion), whereas antisymmetric components (e.g. the gradient $\nabla = [-1,1]$) operate analogously to the momentum term $pc$ in generally inducing a displacement (e.g. vibration or translation). For typical small discrete filters, this displacement is determined by the ratio of antisymmetric to total filter energy, analogously to how the displacement of a relativistic particle relates to a Lorentz transform with beta parameter $β= \frac{v}{c}=\frac{pc}{E}$ equal to the ratio of momentum $pc$ to total energy $E$.
  Repeated filtering leads to the Gaussian scale-space and emergent scale-invariant features. These constructions share a Laplacian-driven structure with the classical heat (diffusion) equation and, via standard mathematical correspondences, with the Schrödinger equation and aspects of the Friedmann equations, together with emergent Morse topological structure. Demonstrations in 3D images reveal blob-like, scale-invariant Morse critical points in images spanning a wide range of physical scales, including organic sugar molecules and inorganic silicon crystals, human and primate brains in magnetic resonance images (MRI), galaxies and the cosmic microwave background (CMB).

</details>


### [6] [Thinking on Shots: Consistent Multi-Shot Video Editing with Agentic Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26809)
*Chenyang Wu,Fuchen Long,Binyuan Huang,Xinlong Sun,Xi Chen,Chun-Le Guo,Chongyi Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: While generative AI has significantly advanced video editing, existing methods primarily focus on single-shot or short video clips. Editing long videos with multiple instructions remains a formidable challenge. Naive chunking strategies, e.g., fixed-duration segmentation, often lead to entity fragmentation, severe editing hallucinations, and disrupted temporal continuity. To bridge this gap, we introduce the Multi-Instruction Multi-Shot Long-Video Editing (MMLVE) task, which is structured around three core objectives: Cross-Shot Editing Consistency (CSEC), Multi-Instruction Decoupling (MID), and Zero-Destruction on Spatiotemporal Structure (ZDSS). To tackle these three unique challenges, we introduce an agentic editing framework that leverages the synergy of Large Language Models (LLMs) and Vision-Language Models (VLMs) to achieve shot-level video decoupling and precise instruction parsing. Furthermore, to comprehensively evaluate this task, we construct MMLVE-Bench, which is an MMLVE-focused dataset characterized by complex real-world spatiotemporal dynamics, high-density heterogeneous instructions, and sparse, random entity distributions. Three MMLVE-focused evaluation metrics are further exploited to assess the quality of the editing results. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our MMLVE-Agent outperforms existing closed-source SOTA approaches (e.g., Seedance 2.0), successfully eliminating editing hallucinations, preserving cross-shot editing consistency, and attaining seamless spatiotemporal transitions.

</details>


### [7] [VIPER: An Expert-Curated Benchmark for Vision-Language Models in Veterinary Pathology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26382)
*Luca L. Weishaupt,Simone de Brot,Javier Asin,Llorenç Grau-Roma,Nic G. Reitsam,Andrew H. Song,Dongmin Bang,Stefan T. Kaluziak,Long Phi Le,Jakob Nikolas Kather,Faisal Mahmood,Guillaume Jaume*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VIPER is the first expert-curated benchmark for evaluating vision-language models in toxicologic pathology, addressing the gap where existing benchmarks focus only on human oncology. It contains 1,251 questions across 419 rat histology images covering seven organ systems, curated by board-certified veterinary pathologists.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing pathology vision-language benchmarks are limited to human tissue, particularly oncology, leaving non-human pathology unaddressed. This gap is particularly important in toxicologic pathology, where microscopic examination of laboratory animal tissues is essential for preclinical drug safety assessment.

Method: The benchmark was created with 1,251 questions associated with 419 H&E-stained rat histology images across seven organ systems, covering multiple-choice, KPrim, and free-text formats. All questions were curated and validated by board-certified veterinary pathologists. Sixteen models were benchmarked, including two veterinary-pathology models, seven human pathology-specialized models, and seven general-purpose frontier models.

Result: The study identifies a substantial domain gap between veterinary and human pathology, exposes the risk of over-diagnosis of normal tissue in frontier models, and demonstrates that domain-specific training remains critical for visually grounded predictions.

Conclusion: The domain gap between veterinary and human pathology is substantial, and domain-specific training is essential for vision-language models in toxicologic pathology applications. The VIPER benchmark provides a valuable tool for evaluating and improving models in this domain.

Abstract: Pathology vision-language models are advancing rapidly, yet existing benchmarks remain focused on human tissue, particularly oncology, leaving non-human pathology largely unaddressed. This gap is especially important in toxicologic pathology, where microscopic tissue examination of laboratory animals is a core component of preclinical drug safety assessment. To address it, we introduce VIPER, the first expert-curated benchmark for vision-language model evaluation in toxicologic pathology. VIPER contains 1,251 questions associated with 419 H&E-stained rat histology images across seven organ systems, covering multiple-choice, KPrim, and free-text formats. All questions were curated and validated by board-certified veterinary pathologists. In total, we benchmarked 16 models, including two newly introduced veterinary-pathology models, seven human pathology-specialized models, and seven general-purpose frontier models. The results identify a substantial domain gap between veterinary and human pathology, expose the risk of over-diagnosis of normal tissue in frontier models, and show that domain-specific training remains critical for visually grounded predictions. VIPER data and evaluation code are available at https://github.com/mahmoodlab/viper.

</details>


### [8] [Mapping Woody Vegetation from Multi-Source Imagery and Prediction Fusion for Enhanced Data Efficiency and Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26471)
*Kal Backman,Jared Wood,Adam Roff*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a deep learning framework for mapping woody vegetation (trees >2m height) across New South Wales, Australia. The approach addresses two key limitations of deep learning: vulnerability to variable image quality and need for large annotated datasets. The framework uses image normalization and prediction fusion for robustness, and label transfer from multiple image sources for data efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Tree cover maps are essential for ecological insights, change detection, vegetation mapping, and fire monitoring. However, reliable mapping requires cloud-free imagery and large amounts of human-annotated training data, which are both significant limitations in practice.

Method: The framework employs three key methods: (1) Image composition method that normalizes imagery and removes defects to improve robustness against varying image quality; (2) Prediction fusion method to minimize reliance on individual image quality; (3) Label transfer from multiple image sources as data augmentation to improve data efficiency.

Result: The image composition method achieved 38.2% error reduction. The prediction fusion method achieved 53.6% error reduction. Multi-source learning from different image sources showed the largest improvement, with error reduction ranging from 28.1% to 76.2% across validation experiments, and reduced performance standard deviation across image dates by a factor of 13.

Conclusion: Data fusion techniques, including image composition, prediction fusion, and multi-source learning through label transfer, significantly improve the data efficiency and robustness of deep learning models for tree cover mapping. The approach reduces errors substantially while also making models more stable across different image acquisition dates.

Abstract: Tree cover maps are a fundamental remote sensing product, used to derive ecological insights about the landscape and are essential to change detection, vegetation mapping and fire monitoring programs. However, comprehensive tree cover mapping requires reliable and high-quality imagery, free of cloud and weather defects to ensure accurate model outputs. Deep learning approaches can generate high quality maps with minimal human intervention but require large amounts of human annotated data to be successful. In this work we propose a framework consisting of methods that aim to improve the data efficiency and robustness of deep learning models using data fusion techniques to segment woody vegetation defined as vegetation over the height of 2m across the state of New South Wales, Australia. To improve robustness against varying image quality, we propose an image composition method that normalizes the imagery and removes defects, whilst also minimizing the reliance on individual image quality by proposing a prediction fusion method. The two methods resulted in an error reduction of 38.2% and 53.6% respectively compared to single-source imagery. To address deep learning approaches' limitation of requiring large amounts of data, we apply label transfer to multiple sources of imagery as a form of data augmentation to improve data efficiency. Learning from multiple image sources was shown to be the biggest improvement in performance, resulting in an error reduction between 28.1% to 76.2% across the different validation experiments, whilst reducing the standard deviation of performance across image dates by a factor of 13.

</details>


### [9] [TempJail: Temporal Jailbreak Attacks against Image-to-Video Generation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26971)
*Qi Lu,Zehui Guo,David Yuanda Gan,Zijing Li,Hengda Zhang,Weijun Xu,Qiankun Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces TempJail, a temporal jailbreak framework that exploits a novel vulnerability in image-to-video (I2V) generation models where unsafe content emerges from semantic composition over time, rather than single frames. The attack bypasses safety filters by decomposing malicious intent into benign initial frames and temporally evolving text instructions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing jailbreak attacks on video generation models only consider single-frame violations, ignoring the unique temporal dimension of I2V systems. The authors discover that unsafe semantics can emerge through semantic composition over time, representing a new attack surface that current safety mechanisms fail to address.

Method: The proposed TempJail framework addresses two key challenges: (1) Temporal abstraction - decomposing malicious captions into initial frame visual conditions and temporal text instructions; (2) Semantic camouflage - modeling semantic injection as controlled latent perturbation with gradient guidance from pretrained encoders on the image side, while rewriting captions into innocuous "subject-action-scene" templates on the text side.

Result: TempJail achieves a 23.3% improvement in attack success rate over prior state-of-the-art methods under GPT-5.2 evaluation, and a 22.0% improvement under human evaluation, when tested on commercial models including Kling, Seedance, Veo, and PixVerse.

Conclusion: This work reveals a critical temporal vulnerability in I2V generation systems and demonstrates that safety mechanisms must consider temporal semantic composition, not just per-frame content. The proposed framework successfully exploits this vulnerability while bypassing existing safety filters through multi-modal semantic camouflage.

Abstract: In recent years, image-to-video (I2V) generation models have made remarkable progress in subject consistency and temporal coherence, enabling high quality video synthesis. However, these advances also introduce new safety risks. Existing studies mainly focus on jailbreak attacks involving single frame violations, while largely overlooking the temporal dimension unique to video generation models. In this paper, we investigate three attack scenarios and uncover a temporal vulnerability in I2V systems: unsafe semantics may emerge not from a single frame, but from semantic composition over time. We further identify two key challenges in such attacks: temporal abstraction and semantic camouflage. To address these issues, we propose TempJail, a novel temporal jailbreak framework for I2V systems. For temporal abstraction, we decompose a target malicious caption into an initial frame visual condition and a temporal text instruction. For semantic camouflage, on the image side we model semantic injection as controlled latent perturbation in diffusion sampling and introduce gradient guidance from pretrained encoders. On the text side, we rewrite the caption into an innocuous ``subject-action-scene'' template that bypasses safety filters while preserving temporal guidance. In the black-box inference phase, these two modalities jointly enable malicious semantics to be gradually triggered over time. Experiments on closed-source commercial models, including Kling, Seedance, Veo and PixVerse, show that TempJail improves attack success rate over prior state-of-the-art methods by 23.3\% under GPT-5.2 evaluation and 22.0\% under human evaluation. Our codes are available at \href{https://github.com/luqi-glory/TempJail}{GitHub}.

</details>


### [10] [Zero-Shot Video Restoration and Enhancement with Text-to-Image Latent Diffusion Models and Multi-Modal References](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26476)
*Cong Cao,Huanjing Yue,Xin Liu,Jingyu Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A zero-shot video restoration framework using text-to-image latent diffusion models with multi-modal references. The method addresses temporal flickering issues through dual prompt tuning inversion/sampling, texture-aware video token merging, and referenced self-attention mechanisms, achieving 3x faster inference and superior temporal consistency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Zero-shot image restoration methods using text-to-image latent diffusion models have achieved success in universal image restoration without training. However, applying these methods directly to video restoration results in severe temporal flickering, indicating a need for temporal consistency improvements in video restoration tasks.

Method: The paper proposes: (1) Dual prompt tuning inversion and sampling for reduced inference time (~1/3 of original); (2) Texture-aware video token merging to utilize temporal correlation between frames; (3) Referenced self-attention and referenced token merging to support image reference for multi-modal input.

Result: The proposed method significantly reduces inference time to approximately 1/3 of the original while strengthening performance and temporal consistency. Experimental results demonstrate superiority in restoring and enhancing temporally consistent videos.

Conclusion: The framework successfully enables zero-shot video restoration using text-to-image latent diffusion models with improved temporal consistency. The combination of dual prompt tuning, texture-aware token merging, and referenced mechanisms provides an effective solution for universal video restoration without training.

Abstract: Zero-shot image restoration methods with text-to-image latent diffusion models have achieved great success in universal image restoration tasks without training. However, applying them to video restoration will result in severe temporal flickering. In this paper, we propose a novel framework for zero-shot video restoration and enhancement which uses a text-to-image latent diffusion model and multi-modal references. Through the proposed dual prompt tuning inversion and sampling, the inference time can be reduced to nearly 1/3 of the original. The performance and temporal consistency can be also significantly stregthened. By using the proposed texture-aware video token merging, the temporal correlation between frames can be further utilized to improve the temporal consistency. We futher propose the referenced self-attention and referenced token merging to support image reference. Experimental results demonstrate the superiority of the proposed method in restoring and enhancing temporally consistent videos.

</details>


### [11] [Learning Woody Clearing With Loss Alignment for Zero-Shot Regrowth and Woody Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26489)
*Kal Backman,Jared Wood,Adam Roff*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents a deep learning approach for detecting woody vegetation change (clearing and regrowth) from bitemporal Sentinel-2 satellite imagery across New South Wales, Australia. The key innovation is a loss scaling coefficient α that aligns the model's objective with end-user Fβ metrics. The method also uses input augmentation and generation techniques to enable zero-shot transfer to regrowth detection and woody segmentation tasks, achieving significant improvements including 1.85x precision increase, 1.12x recall increase, 18.2% error reduction in segmentation, and 0.845 F1 score for regrowth detection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deep learning models for woody change detection may not meet end-user specifications due to misaligned loss definitions. Additionally, detecting spatially rare and ambiguous events like regrowth is challenging due to limited large datasets. The study aims to address these limitations by introducing a loss scaling coefficient to optimize for specific Fβ scores and developing techniques to enable zero-shot transfer to related tasks without requiring task-specific training data.

Method: The study uses bitemporal Sentinel-2 imagery spanning 7 years across New South Wales, Australia. A loss scaling coefficient α is introduced to transform the objective function to optimize for specific Fβ scores. For zero-shot transfer, the method employs: 1) input imagery augmentation and generation techniques including activation maximization with low α values for stability, and 2) image generation techniques using handcrafted features with a mosaic of clearing patches and artificial trees for contextual grounding.

Result: Introducing the loss scaling coefficient α increased precision by 1.85x or recall by 1.12x depending on the target metric. For woody segmentation, the image generation techniques outperformed prior works, reducing overall error by up to 18.2%. For zero-shot woody regrowth detection, creating pseudo-post and prior images enabled the model to achieve an F1 score of 0.845.

Conclusion: The proposed loss scaling coefficient α effectively aligns deep learning model objectives with end-user metrics for woody change detection. The input augmentation and generation techniques successfully enable zero-shot transfer to regrowth detection and woody segmentation tasks without task-specific training, establishing a foundation for future regrowth detection work. The method demonstrates practical applicability for biodiversity management through improved woody vegetation monitoring.

Abstract: Detecting woody clearing is vital for managing biodiversity. Deep learning models can detect change in woody vegetation from bitemporal remote sensing imagery, however generated products may not meet end-user specifications due to unaligned loss definitions. Further limitations of deep learning models are the reliance on large datasets which can be difficult to attain for spatially rare and ambiguous events such as regrowth detection. In this work we train a model to detect woody change using bitemporal Sentinel-2 imagery consisting of 7 years' worth of annual imagery across the state of New South Wales, Australia. To align the objective of the model with end-user metrics, we introduce the loss scaling coefficient $α$ which transforms the objective to optimize for specific $F_β$ scores. Introducing $α$ was found to increase precision by 1.85x or recall by 1.12x. We propose input imagery augmentation and generation techniques that allow the woody change detection model to zero-shot transfer to regrowth and woody segmentation tasks. For woody segmentation, image generation techniques using activation maximization with low $α$ values for stability and image generation techniques derived from handcrafted features utilizing a mosaic of clearing patches and artificial trees for contextual grounding were found to outperform prior woody segmentation works of the study area, reducing the overall error by up to 18.2%. For zero-shot woody regrowth, creating pseudo-post and prior images resulted in the model achieving an F1 score of 0.845, creating a foundation for future regrowth detection work.

</details>


### [12] [Video-FLAIR: Not Whether to Reason, But How](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26495)
*Yogesh Kulkarni,Pooyan Fazli*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Video-FLAIR is a training framework that enables multimodal language models to adaptively select the appropriate reasoning mode (perceptual, compositional, or deliberative) for each query using reinforcement learning, achieving improved accuracy while significantly reducing computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Multimodal queries require different types of reasoning, but existing methods apply uniform reasoning strategies across all queries, causing inefficiency on simple tasks and insufficient reasoning on complex ones.

Method: Uses reinforcement learning to train the model to select appropriate reasoning mode. During training, generates responses under all three modes (perceptual, compositional, deliberative) for the same prompt. A composite reward compares responses based on correctness, grounding, and cost, while discouraging unsupported or misaligned deliberation, providing supervision without per-query annotations.

Result: Improves accuracy over Qwen2.5-VL base model by +5.4 on MathVista, +4.8 on Video-Holmes, and +4.8 on Video-MMMU, while reducing average token usage from 417 to 95 (77% reduction).

Conclusion: Adaptive reasoning selection through RL enables efficient allocation of computational resources—using simple reasoning for straightforward queries and complex reasoning only when needed—achieving better accuracy with substantially fewer tokens.

Abstract: Multimodal queries can require different types of reasoning. Some can be answered via perceptual reasoning, extracting information directly from the visual signal, while others require compositional reasoning that combines observations or deliberative reasoning that evaluates competing hypotheses. However, many existing methods apply a uniform reasoning strategy across queries, leading to unnecessary computation on simple tasks and insufficient reasoning on complex ones. We introduce Video-FLAIR, a training framework that learns to select the appropriate reasoning mode for each query using reinforcement learning. During training, the model generates responses under all three modes for the same prompt, enabling direct comparison. A composite reward compares these responses to favor the most effective one based on correctness, grounding, and cost, while discouraging unsupported or misaligned deliberation. This yields a supervision signal for learning adaptive reasoning without per-query annotations. Video-FLAIR improves accuracy over the Qwen2.5-VL base model by +5.4 on MathVista, +4.8 on Video-Holmes, and +4.8 on Video-MMMU, while reducing average token usage to 95 compared to 417 for always-thinking baselines.

</details>


### [13] [Systematic Literature Review of Machine Learning Models and Applications for Text Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26500)
*Nuzhat Khan,Ab Al-Hadi Ab Rahman,Shahriyar Masud Rizvi,Ibrahim Yousef Alshareef,Muhammad Nadzir Marsono,Muhammad Paend Bakht,Mohd Shahrizal Rusli,Shahidatul Sadiah*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Optical Character Recognition (OCR) for text recognition using machine vision has significantly improved, particularly when handling heterogeneous textual data. Traditional OCR models struggle with script variations, writing styles, and degraded documents. Advancements in technology are leading to new AI models with improved architecture for handling multiple languages and complex data formats. Despite this progress, a comprehensive evaluation of OCR advancements remains limited. Based on the established preferred reporting items for systematic reviews and meta-analysis (PRISMA) guidelines, this literature review presents an extensive assessment of OCR research to trace the evolution of AI models over the past decade. It explores the transition in AI models, application domains, data types, linguistic coverage, and challenges. Through a detailed analysis of 97 selected studies published during January 2015 - January 2025, key OCR models are identified, and their performance, strengths, and limitations are analyzed. The findings highlight how OCR technologies have evolved to address structured and unstructured text, scene text recognition, and multilingual processing. Unresolved challenges include limited resources for underrepresented languages, high variability in handwritten text, visual similarity among characters, and constraints in real-time OCR applications. To address these issues, several promising approaches are proposed. Key suggestions include self-supervised learning, multimodal AI, automated machine learning (AutoML), AI-assisted postprocessing, tiny machine learning (TinyML), and the creation of joint corpora for script matching. The future recommendations aim to enhance OCR accuracy and tackle the challenges identified for real-time industrial applications. This study will guide future research and establish a foundation for OCR field.

</details>


### [14] [NeuDonatello: Uncertainty-Aware Framework for Accurate Neural SDF Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26504)
*Alvin Jinsung Choi,Wanhee Kim,Taeyun Kim,Dasol Hong,Wooju Lee,Hyun Myung*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: NeuDonatello is a novel neural surface reconstruction framework that models and leverages signed distance function (SDF) uncertainty to improve 3D surface recovery from multi-view RGB images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recovering accurate geometry solely from RGB images remains challenging due to uncertainties from textureless regions, occlusions, and inherent scene ambiguities. Existing methods overlook these uncertainties, leading to inaccurate SDF estimates.

Method: The approach models spatially varying uncertainty using Monte Carlo sampling, develops adaptive regularization that selectively strengthens geometric constraints where RGB supervision is unreliable, and introduces an uncertainty-aware scale parameter for SDF-to-density conversion to model spatially varying densities.

Result: NeuDonatello achieves state-of-the-art reconstruction accuracy with robust performance across diverse scenes using only posed RGB images.

Conclusion: Explicitly modeling and leveraging SDF uncertainty enables more accurate surface reconstruction, particularly in challenging scenarios with textureless regions and occlusions.

Abstract: Neural surface reconstruction has emerged as a powerful paradigm for recovering high-quality 3D surfaces from multi-view images. However, recovering accurate geometry solely from RGB images remains challenging due to uncertainties arising from textureless regions, occlusions, and inherent scene ambiguities. Existing methods often overlook such uncertainties, leading to inaccurate estimates of the signed distance function (SDF). We introduce NeuDonatello, a novel framework that models and leverages SDF uncertainty to improve surface reconstruction. Central to our approach is to model spatially varying uncertainty using a Monte Carlo sampling strategy. Using this uncertainty, we develop an adaptive regularization that selectively strengthens geometric constraints where RGB supervision is unreliable, avoiding incorrect surface reconstruction. We further introduce an uncertainty-aware scale parameter for the SDF-to-density conversion. Conditioned on uncertainty, this design enables more accurate modeling of spatially varying densities. Extensive experiments demonstrate that NeuDonatello achieves state-of-the-art reconstruction accuracy, with robust performance across diverse scenes using only posed RGB images.

</details>


### [15] [HUG-VIS: A Multimodal Benchmark for Human-centered Understanding and Generation in Visual Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26517)
*Fei Ma,Zebang Cheng,Minghui Li,Hongbo Xu,Yuyong Tan,Yihua Shao,Hanling Wang,Zhou Liu,Yuqing Gao,Dong Wang,Long Ma,Laizhong Cui,Nicu Sebe,Qi Tian*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HUG-VIS is a unified benchmark for human-centered visual intelligence that addresses the lack of integrated resources for studying people as expressive, socially situated subjects. It contains 8,400 videos with synchronized modalities (video, audio, text, alpha mattes) from 30 actors performing 280 emotion-action prompts, evaluating models across four tasks: emotion recognition, video generation, voice cloning, and video matting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visual intelligence resources are task-specific, providing modalities and annotations for individual problems rather than a shared foundation. This limits multimodal signal use and broader research on human-centered understanding and generation.

Method: The benchmark uses a controlled Mandarin studio protocol with 30 professional actors performing 280 emotion-action-prompt assignments. Diverse open- and closed-source models are evaluated under a unified zero-shot protocol using automatic metrics, criterion-specific mean opinion scores, and multiple cross-task analyses.

Result: (i) Linguistic content dominates current emotion recognition while purely visual affect recognition is weakest; (ii) In video generation and voice cloning, automatic metrics and human judgment agree overall but differ in top rankings, requiring joint reporting; (iii) Boundary fidelity under motion is the main remaining obstacle for human matting; (iv) Task difficulty varies across emotions, models, and metrics with notable cross-task correlations.

Conclusion: HUG-VIS provides a unified benchmark enabling comprehensive evaluation of human-centered visual intelligence across understanding and generation tasks. The findings highlight the importance of linguistic content in emotion recognition, the need for joint metric and human evaluation, and that boundary fidelity under motion remains a key challenge for human matting.

Abstract: Visual intelligence seeks to perceive, interpret, and synthesize the visual world and is central to modern computer vision. Human-centered visual intelligence is especially demanding because it studies people as expressive, socially situated subjects whose meaning is rarely conveyed by appearance alone. It couples vision with audio and language across four representative tasks: human emotion recognition, human video generation, human voice cloning, and human video matting. Yet existing resources remain task-specific, providing modalities and annotations for individual problems rather than a shared foundation coordinating understanding and generation. This limits multimodal signal use and broader research. We address this gap with HUG-VIS, a unified benchmark for Human-centered Understanding and Generation in Visual Intelligence. It contains 8,400 seated half-body videos of 30 professional actors, each performing the same 280 emotion-action-prompt assignments under a controlled Mandarin studio protocol, with synchronized video, audio, text, and alpha mattes. We evaluate diverse open- and closed-source models across the four tasks under a unified zero-shot protocol using automatic metrics, criterion-specific mean opinion scores, and multiple cross-task analyses. Results show that (i) linguistic content dominates current emotion recognition, while purely visual affect recognition is weakest; (ii) in video generation and voice cloning, automatic metrics and human judgment agree overall but differ in their top rankings, requiring joint reporting; (iii) boundary fidelity under motion is the main remaining obstacle for human matting; and (iv) task difficulty varies across emotions, models, and metrics, with notable cross-task correlations. The dataset and results are available at https://github.com/GML-MMGroup/HUG-VIS.

</details>


### [16] [FAN-LoRA: A Fourier-Adaptive Nonlinear Low-Rank Adaptor for Medical Foundation Model Domain Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26531)
*Ziquan Liu,Zhewei Zhu,Xuyang Shi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces FAN-LoRA, a novel frequency-decoupled fine-tuning method that adapts the Segment Anything Model (SAM) to medical imaging by separating low-pass (global structure) and high-pass (local texture) branches. It addresses domain shift challenges in cross-modality and cross-center medical image segmentation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Vision foundation models like SAM face significant performance degradation when directly applied to medical imaging due to domain gaps (cross-modality and cross-center shifts). Existing PEFT methods suffer from performance degradation under severe distribution shifts because heterogeneous frequency components are entangled in a shared low-rank subspace, causing sub-optimal structural alignment and blurred boundaries.

Method: The proposed FAN-LoRA uses a frequency-decoupled architecture with two branches: (1) a B-spline-driven low-pass branch for capturing global structural alignment, and (2) a discrete Fourier high-pass branch for local textural compensation. This separation explicitly decouples the optimization space for different frequency components.

Result: Extensive experiments on three challenging cross-modality and cross-center benchmarks show that FAN-LoRA consistently outperforms state-of-the-art PEFT baselines. It achieves improved average Dice scores and notable reductions in boundary errors while maintaining compact module size and computational efficiency.

Conclusion: FAN-LoRA successfully addresses the representational bottleneck in adapting SAM to medical imaging by decoupling frequency components. The frequency-adaptive approach provides a more effective representation for handling distribution shifts between natural and medical images, demonstrating the importance of explicitly separating different frequency components in low-rank adaptation methods.

Abstract: The advent of vision foundation models, notably the Segment Anything Model (SAM), has catalyzed significant advancements in natural image segmentation. However, their direct transfer to medical imaging remains severely bottlenecked by profound domain gaps, such as cross-modality and cross-center shifts. Existing Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) methods facilitate the adaptation of SAM to medical domains; nevertheless, they frequently suffer from performance degradation under severe distribution shifts. This vulnerability primarily stems from the implicit entanglement of heterogeneous frequency components within a shared low-rank subspace, which directly exacerbates sub-optimal structural alignment and localized boundary blurring. To overcome this representational bottleneck, we propose the Fourier-Adaptive Nonlinear Low-Rank Adaptor (FAN-LoRA), a novel frequency-decoupled fine-tuning architecture. FAN-LoRA explicitly separates the optimization space by employing a B-spline-driven low-pass branch for global structural alignment, synergistically coupled with a discrete Fourier high-pass branch for local textural compensation. Extensive experiments across three challenging cross-modality and cross-center benchmarks demonstrate that FAN-LoRA consistently outperforms state-of-the-art PEFT baselines. Compared to the strongest competitors, our method achieves consistent improvements in average Dice scores and notable reductions in boundary errors, while maintaining a compact module size without compromising computational efficiency.

</details>


### [17] [Visual Information-Guided Parallel Decoding for Diffusion Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26580)
*Insu Lee,Wooje Park,Wonseok Shin,Jinwoo Son,Byonghyo Shim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VIG-Sampler is a new token decoding strategy for diffusion multimodal large language models (dMLLMs) that prioritizes tokens based on their attention to image tokens, with an additional constraint to penalize tokens whose image-attention distributions are similar to previously selected tokens, thereby increasing information gain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current dMLLMs use either certainty-based or influence-based token selection strategies during decoding. However, certainty-based methods favor frequently observed tokens, while influence-based methods don't explicitly account for the input image. This work aims to leverage visual information to guide token selection for better multimodal generation quality.

Method: The VIG-Sampler prioritizes tokens based on their attention to image tokens. Additionally, it imposes a constraint that penalizes candidate tokens whose image-attention distributions are similar to those of previously selected tokens, thereby increasing information gain of the decoded subset.

Result: VIG-Sampler outperforms the Info-Gain Sampler by an average of 19.3 CIDEr points across captioning benchmarks and surpasses it on COCO Caption while using only half as many decoding steps.

Conclusion: The proposed Visual Information-Guided Sampler effectively improves multimodal generation quality by leveraging visual attention information, demonstrating significant improvements over existing sampling methods with fewer decoding steps.

Abstract: Diffusion multimodal large language models (dMLLMs) have recently emerged as a new decoding paradigm for multimodal generation. Starting from a fully masked sequence, dMLLMs progressively decode the sequence by unmasking a subset of the remaining masked positions at each step. Since the selected tokens serve as the prediction context for subsequent steps, deciding which tokens to decode is crucial to the quality of the final output. The most common strategy prioritizes tokens based on a certainty measure that tends to favor tokens frequently observed in the training data. Recent approaches instead order tokens according to their influence on subsequent predictions, but do not explicitly account for the input image. We propose the Visual Information-Guided Sampler (VIG-Sampler), which prioritizes tokens based on their attention to image tokens. We further impose a constraint that penalizes candidate tokens whose image-attention distributions are similar to those of previously selected tokens, thereby increasing the information gain of the decoded subset. Extensive experiments on 7 captioning and VQA benchmarks with 3 open-source dMLLMs demonstrate the effectiveness of VIG-Sampler, which outperforms the Info-Gain Sampler by an average of 19.3 CIDEr points across the captioning benchmarks and surpasses it on COCO Caption while using only half as many decoding steps.

</details>


### [18] [DPA-I2P: Depth-Guided Projective Alignment for Image-to-Point-Cloud Registration in Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26589)
*Wenxin Zhang,Hang Li,Zhiwei Xu,Qiankun Dong,Gang Wang,Tao Li*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: DPA-I2P is a novel depth-guided method for image-to-point cloud registration in autonomous driving. It addresses cross-modal correspondence learning challenges between images and sparse LiDAR point clouds through three key components: RMDE for geometry-aware depth encoding, PVL for consistent vision lifting, and CQP for suppressing unreliable queries. The method achieves significant improvements on KITTI (45.0% RTE reduction, 55.6% RRE reduction) and nuScenes datasets over implicit baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Image-to-point cloud registration is fundamental for autonomous driving and large-scale outdoor localization. Recent implicit correspondence learning methods have improved registration performance through end-to-end cross-modal alignment. However, the inherent modality discrepancy between images and sparse LiDAR point clouds makes reliable cross-modal correspondence learning challenging.

Method: The proposed DPA-I2P method consists of three key components: (1) Ray-Conditioned Metric Depth Encoding (RMDE) - exploits depth and visual cues in a structured, geometry-aware manner; (2) Projection-Consistent Vision Lifting (PVL) - also exploits depth and visual cues in a geometry-aware way; (3) Cross-Modal Query Pruning (CQP) - suppresses unreliable queries during early refinement to improve matching stability.

Result: On KITTI dataset, DPA-I2P reduces RTE (Relative Translation Error) and RRE (Relative Rotation Error) by 45.0% and 55.6% respectively over the strongest implicit baseline. On nuScenes dataset, DPA-I2P also improves registration accuracy over all evaluated baselines, demonstrating better transferability to different driving scenes.

Conclusion: The proposed DPA-I2P method effectively addresses the cross-modal correspondence learning challenge in image-to-point cloud registration. By using depth-guided techniques (RMDE, PVL) and query pruning (CQP), the method achieves state-of-the-art performance on both KITTI and nuScenes datasets, with particularly strong transferability across different driving scenarios.

Abstract: Image-to-Point Cloud Registration aims to estimate the camera pose of a given image within a 3D scene point cloud, which is a fundamental task in autonomous driving and large-scale outdoor localization. Recent implicit correspondence learning methods have improved registration performance by learning cross-modal alignment in an end-to-end framework, leading to more accurate camera pose estimation. However, due to the inherent modality discrepancy between images and sparse LiDAR point clouds, reliable cross-modal correspondence learning remains challenging. To address this issue, we propose Depth-Guided Projective Alignment for Image-to-Point-Cloud Registration (DPA-I2P). Unlike naive depth or feature concatenation, Ray-Conditioned Metric Depth Encoding (RMDE) and Projection-Consistent Vision Lifting (PVL) exploit depth and visual cues in a structured, geometry-aware manner. In addition, Cross-Modal Query Pruning (CQP) suppresses unreliable queries during early refinement to improve matching stability. Experiments on KITTI and nuScenes demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method. On KITTI, DPA-I2P reduces RTE and RRE by 45.0% and 55.6% over the strongest implicit baseline, respectively. On nuScenes, DPA-I2P also improves registration accuracy over the evaluated baselines, suggesting better transferability to different driving scenes.

</details>


### [19] [FU-Mamba: A Frequency-Enhanced Dynamic Scanning Framework for Oralscan Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26607)
*Xinxin Zhao,Jinpeng Ye,Bo Wei,Liqin Wu,Mahmoud Hassaballah,Karen Egiazarian,Aura Conci,Victor Hugo C. de Albuquerque,Abdulkadir Sengur,Leszek Rutkowski,Yan Tian*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FU-Mamba introduces a novel visual state space model for oral scan image segmentation in digital dentistry, featuring dynamic content-aware scanning and frequency domain enhancement to improve segmentation accuracy under challenging imaging conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visual SSMs rely on manually designed scanning orders that disrupt semantic spatial continuity and hinder coherent feature extraction. Additionally, adverse imaging conditions (inconsistent lighting, reflective surfaces, noise) diminish high-frequency details while enhancing low-frequency components, hindering accurate boundary localization.

Method: FU-Mamba incorporates two key components: (1) Dynamic Mamba Block (DMB) that adaptively learns sampling offsets via a trainable offset prediction network and performs flexible bilinear interpolation for content-aware scanning that preserves spatial coherence; (2) A frequency domain enhancement block that balances spectral components through wavelet-guided decomposition and spectrum pooling, improving robustness under adverse imaging conditions.

Result: FU-Mamba achieves a 1.1% increase in mean intersection over union (mIoU) on the dental segmentation dataset, demonstrating notable enhancement in segmentation accuracy.

Conclusion: The proposed FU-Mamba framework effectively addresses the challenges of semantic spatial continuity preservation and frequency domain degradation in oral scan image segmentation, providing a promising solution for computer-aided diagnosis and treatment planning in digital dentistry.

Abstract: Oralscan image segmentation is essential for computer-aided diagnosis and treatment planning in digital dentistry. However, existing visual state space models (SSMs) often rely on manually designed scanning orders to flatten image patches into sequences, which disrupts the semantic spatial continuity and hinders coherent feature extraction from key foreground regions. Moreover, elements such as inconsistent lighting, reflective surfaces, and noise during data acquisition disrupt the frequency distribution by diminishing high-frequency details while enhancing low-frequency components, consequently hindering the accurate localization of boundaries. In response to these challenges, we introduce FU-Mamba, an innovative framework that incorporates dynamic scanning and frequency domain enhancement within the SSM architecture. Specifically, the Dynamic Mamba Block (DMB) adaptively learns sampling offsets via a trainable offset prediction network and performs flexible bilinear interpolation, enabling content-aware scanning that preserves spatial coherence. Furthermore, a frequency domain enhancement block balances spectral components through wavelet-guided decomposition and spectrum pooling, improving robustness under adverse imaging conditions. Experimental findings indicate that FU-Mamba attains a notable enhancement in segmentation accuracy, evidenced by a 1.1% increase in the mean intersection over union (mIoU) metric when evaluated on the dental segmentation dataset. Project page: https://byte2bite.github.io/FU-Mamba/

</details>


### [20] [Text-to-seed generation: Training-free open-vocabulary seeded semantic segmentation via re-purposing diffusion as text-guided seed generator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26624)
*Kumju Jo,Heesun Jung,Sungyong Baik*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes Text-to-Seed (T2S), a training-free framework for open-vocabulary semantic segmentation that uses Stable Diffusion to generate attention-based seed points from text queries, then feeds these seeds to SAM for region expansion. This approach reformulates OVSS as text-guided seed localization followed by seed-based expansion, avoiding reliance on inaccurate coarse masks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: SAM alone has limited performance on OVSS. Existing methods use SAM to refine coarse masks from other models, but this strategy fails when initial masks are inaccurate. The authors argue more reliable segmentation can be achieved by using accurate object points (seeds) rather than coarse masks to guide SAM.

Method: T2S leverages the text-to-region correspondence of Stable Diffusion to generate attention-based seed points for target categories described by text. These sparse seeds are then used as point prompts for SAM to produce full object masks. The method is training-free and requires no additional annotations.

Result: T2S achieves strong performance on standard OVSS benchmarks without task-specific training or additional annotations, demonstrating the effectiveness of combining semantic grounding (from Stable Diffusion) with seed-driven spatial segmentation (from SAM).

Conclusion: The key insight is that SAM works more reliably as a region expansion module when guided by accurate seed points rather than inaccurate coarse masks. By reformulating OVSS as seeded segmentation and using Stable Diffusion for text-to-seed generation, the method achieves effective open-vocabulary segmentation without any training.

Abstract: Open-vocabulary semantic segmentation (OVSS) aims to segment image regions corresponding to arbitrary text queries. Although the Segment Anything Model (SAM) is a powerful foundation model for segmentation, its standalone performance on OVSS remains limited. Existing methods therefore often use SAM to refine coarse masks predicted by other models, but this strategy is unreliable when the initial masks are inaccurate. In this work, we argue that more reliable segmentation can be achieved by exploiting SAM as a region expansion module guided by accurate object points (i.e., seeds) rather than inaccurate coarse masks. Inspired by classical seeded segmentation, we reformulate OVSS as text-guided seed localization followed by seed-based region expansion. To realize this idea, we propose Text-to-Seed (T2S), a training-free framework that leverages the text-to-region correspondence of Stable Diffusion to generate attention-based seed points for target categories described by text. These sparse seeds are then used as point prompts for SAM to produce full object masks. Without task-specific training or additional annotations, T2S achieves strong performance on standard OVSS benchmarks, demonstrating the effectiveness of combining semantic grounding with seed-driven spatial segmentation.

</details>


### [21] [Who Remains, What Changes: Identity Anchored Composed Gait Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26632)
*Jingchen Fei,Zengbin Wang,Yukun Liu,Muyi Sun,Shibiao Xu,Man Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces Composed Gait Retrieval (CoGR), a new task that retrieves target gait sequences using both a reference sequence and natural language modification queries. The authors propose ComposeGait, an identity-anchored composition framework with a Part-aware Identity Adapter (PIA) to prevent identity drift. They also create the first gait-language datasets using VLMs. The method achieves 72.38% R@1 on Language-Augmented CCPG and 83.61% on Language-Augmented CASIA-B.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing gait recognition methods are limited to rigid visual matching and fail to leverage natural language instructions for interactive retrieval. There is also a lack of datasets for composed gait retrieval tasks.

Method: The authors propose: 1) An automated annotation pipeline using VLMs to construct gait-language datasets; 2) ComposeGait framework with Part-aware Identity Adapter (PIA) that aggregates multi-frame, part-aware identity evidence into sample-specific ID tokens; 3) A shared Q-Former with ID tokens injected into both branches while excluding ID-token outputs from final retrieval embeddings; 4) Joint optimization with identity and task-adapted composed-retrieval objectives.

Result: ComposeGait achieves the best R@1 performance: 72.38% on Language-Augmented CCPG and 83.61% on Language-Augmented CASIA-B, establishing it as a strong baseline for CoGR.

Conclusion: The proposed ComposeGait successfully addresses the CoGR task by preventing identity drift through identity-anchored composition. The new gait-language datasets and framework provide a solid baseline for future research in this direction.

Abstract: Gait recognition has achieved remarkable progress, yet existing methods remain confined to rigid visual matching and often overlook the potential of natural language instructions for interactive retrieval. In this paper, we introduce Composed Gait Retrieval (CoGR), a novel task that retrieves a target gait sequence based on a reference sequence and a natural language modification query. To address the absence of existing datasets for this task, we design an automated annotation pipeline powered by large vision-language models (VLMs) to construct the first gait-language datasets: Language-Augmented CCPG and Language-Augmented CASIA-B. Building on this, we propose ComposeGait, an identity-anchored composition framework designed to prevent the identity drift that arises when generic composed retrieval follows the instruction but returns the wrong person. Its Part-aware Identity Adapter (PIA) aggregates multi-frame, part-aware identity evidence into a sample-specific ID token. We inject the ID tokens into both branches of a shared Q-Former to preserve identity, while excluding the ID-token outputs from the final retrieval embeddings. Joint identity and task-adapted composed-retrieval objectives optimize this space end to end. We evaluate ComposeGait on both benchmarks and show that it achieves the best R@1 among the compared methods, reaching 72.38% on Language-Augmented CCPG and 83.61% on Language-Augmented CASIA-B. These results establish ComposeGait as a strong baseline for CoGR. The datasets and code will be made publicly available.

</details>


### [22] [Real-time Unsupervised Object Discovery from Asynchronous Event Streams](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26644)
*Pratham G. Shenwai,Hemant Kumar Singh,Sridhar Ravi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A lightweight, training-free framework for discovering moving objects from event camera data using spatio-temporal clustering, featuring a linear-time event filter (SPEF) and efficient clustering method (EMCC) that achieves state-of-the-art results without requiring offline training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Event cameras produce highly sparse asynchronous data streams with microsecond resolution, but existing methods for visual perception in latency-critical environments either require expensive training or lack efficiency. This work aims to develop a scalable, training-free solution for discovering moving objects that can operate in resource-constrained settings.

Method: The framework introduces two core contributions: (1) Spatio-temporal Probabilistic Event Filter (SPEF) - a linear-time filter with adaptive event acceptance threshold to distinguish salient motion from background noise; (2) Event Morton Code Clustering (EMCC) - a clustering module that bypasses expensive distance matrix computation to efficiently group events for unsupervised object discovery.

Result: On E-MLB dataset, SPEF achieves best denoising performance among classical filtering methods and remains competitive with learning-based approaches without training. On object discovery, EMCC achieves highest overall accuracy and lowest execution time across FRED and eTraM datasets, substantially outperforming density-based clustering baselines.

Conclusion: This work establishes a new performance benchmark for classical object discovery in event data, providing a highly scalable, training-free solution suitable for resource-constrained visual perception applications.

Abstract: Event cameras capture pixel-level intensity changes with microsecond resolution to produce highly sparse asynchronous data streams. For visual perception in latency-critical environments, we propose a lightweight, training-free framework for discovery of moving objects based on spatio-temporal clustering. This framework is driven by two core contributions. First, a linear-time Spatio-temporal Probabilistic Event Filter (SPEF) that introduces an adaptive event acceptance threshold to distinguish salient motion structures from background noise. Second, an Event Morton Code Clustering (EMCC) module that bypasses expensive distance matrix computation to efficiently group events for unsupervised discovery of moving objects. On the E-MLB dataset benchmark, SPEF achieves the best denoising performance among classical filtering methods and remains competitive with learning-based approaches without requiring any offline training. On object discovery, EMCC achieves the highest overall accuracy and lowest execution time across the FRED and eTraM datasets, outperforming established density-based clustering baselines by a substantial margin. Overall, this work establishes a new performance benchmark for classical object discovery in event data, providing a highly scalable, training-free solution for resource-constrained visual perception. The code is available at https://github.com/PrathamShenwai/SPEF_EMCC

</details>


### [23] [Tissue-Mixture Entropy-Weighted Reconstruction for Partial-Volume-Aware Brain MRI Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26647)
*Xiao Tong,Wenyun Yang,Ziheng Zhang,Jingzhi Han,Zhaochu Luo,Jinbo Yang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper addresses a key limitation in brain MRI super-resolution where tissue-transition regions affected by partial-volume effects (PVE) are underrepresented in standard full-image training objectives. The proposed AGW-PBR method uses tissue-mixture entropy weighting during training to better reconstruct these challenging regions, while only requiring the low-resolution input at inference time.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard full-image objectives in brain MRI super-resolution underweight tissue-transition regions affected by the partial-volume effect (PVE). These regions occupy only a small fraction of the image but are clinically important. Additionally, binary boundaries fail to capture the continuous mixture of cerebrospinal fluid, gray matter, and white matter within voxels.

Method: The proposed AGW-PBR combines: (1) A low-resolution-only reconstruction backbone with Sobel guidance, soft latent-basis assignment, and bounded grid-anchored residual warping. (2) Tissue-mixture entropy derived from quality-controlled tissue fractions from registered T1/T2/PD IXI images, which defines mean-normalized reconstruction weights within validated PVE support. These sidecars are used only during training, while inference requires only the LR image.

Result: AGW-PBR improves full-image reconstruction across 2x, 4x, and 6x IXI scales and enhances regional fidelity at 4x (validated with SynthSeg masks). The PVE-free backbone retains strong performance on fastMRI, demonstrating generalizability.

Conclusion: Tissue-mixture entropy weighting is effective for partial-volume-aware brain MRI super-resolution. The method successfully balances full-image reconstruction quality with tissue-interface fidelity without requiring additional inputs at inference time.

Abstract: Full-image objectives in brain magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) super-resolution (SR) can underweight tissue-transition regions affected by the partial-volume effect (PVE), as these regions occupy only a small fraction of the image. Binary boundaries also do not capture the continuous mixture of cerebrospinal fluid, gray matter, and white matter within a voxel. We propose Anatomy-Guided Gaussian-Parameter Warping with PVE-Balanced Reconstruction (AGW-PBR), which combines a low-resolution (LR)-only reconstruction backbone with a training-time objective that emphasizes tissue transitions. The backbone integrates LR-derived Sobel guidance, soft latent-basis assignment, and bounded grid-anchored residual warping. Fixed, quality-controlled tissue fractions derived from registered T1/T2/PD IXI images are converted into tissue-mixture entropy, which defines mean-normalized reconstruction weights within validated PVE support. These sidecars are used only during training, and inference requires only the LR image. AGW-PBR is evaluated on T2-weighted IXI images at 2x, 4x, and 6x using three seeds and subject-level paired analyses. At 4x, test-only SynthSeg masks independently assess reconstruction in tissue-interface and non-interface regions. Targeted ablations examine valid-support supervision, spatially aligned entropy weighting, and soft latent assignment. The AGW-backbone is also trained from scratch on fastMRI at 4x without PVE supervision. AGW-PBR improves full-image reconstruction across the tested IXI scales and regional fidelity at 4x, while the PVE-free backbone retains strong performance on fastMRI. These findings support tissue-mixture entropy weighting for partial-volume-aware brain MRI SR.

</details>


### [24] [Hierarchical Channel Stacking: A Structured Decision Framework for AI-Generated Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26648)
*Saifullah Shoaib,Akash Borigi,Rupendra Lekkala,Amaury Lendasse,Edward Ratner,Sai Sowjanya Bhamidipati,Alexander Schlager,Peggy Lindner*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HCS (Hierarchical Channel Stacking) is a compact framework for AI-generated image detection that transforms intermediate CNN activations into a structured 60-dimensional representation across three backbone stages. It uses per-channel Level-1 classifiers and a Level-2 aggregator to produce predictions while preserving hierarchical structure for analysis. The method achieves 86.7% accuracy and macro-F1 on a benchmark of GAN and diffusion generated images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Many synthetic-image detectors produce accurate predictions but offer limited insight into how those decisions are formed. The authors aim to address this limitation by creating a structured framework that not only detects AI-generated images but also provides interpretable analysis of how evidence is assembled across representation levels.

Method: HCS converts intermediate CNN activations into a structured 60-dimensional representation organized across three progressively deeper backbone stages. It employs per-channel Level-1 classifiers at each stage and a Level-2 aggregator to produce image-level predictions while preserving explicit hierarchical structure for analysis.

Result: On a benchmark spanning GAN and diffusion generators, HCS achieves 86.7% accuracy and 86.7% macro-F1 on the held-out test set. Stage ablation demonstrates that the full three-stage system outperforms reduced single-stage and two-stage variants, indicating complementary predictive information across stages.
