Stage-level contribution analysis reveals distinct profiles for fake GAN images versus fake diffusion images.

Conclusion: HCS serves not merely as a compact detector but as a structured framework for studying how synthetic-image detectors assemble evidence across representation levels. The hierarchical approach provides interpretability and reveals generator-specific detection patterns, offering insights into the detection mechanisms for different synthesis methods.

Abstract: Many synthetic-image detectors produce accurate predictions but offer limited insight into how those decisions are formed. This paper introduces Hierarchical Channel Stacking (HCS), a compact framework for AI-generated image detection that converts intermediate CNN activations into a structured 60-dimensional representation organized across three progressively deeper backbone stages. HCS uses per-channel Level-1 classifiers and a Level-2 aggregator to produce image-level predictions while preserving explicit hierarchical structure for analysis. On a benchmark spanning GAN and diffusion generators, HCS achieves 86.7% accuracy and 86.7% macro-F1 on the held-out test set. Stage ablation shows that the full three-stage system outperforms reduced single-stage and two-stage variants, indicating that the hierarchy carries complementary predictive information. Stage-level contribution analysis further shows that, in the analyzed detector setting, fake GAN and fake diffusion images exhibit distinct stage-level contribution profiles. These results position HCS not simply as a compact detector, but as a structured framework for studying how synthetic-image detectors assemble evidence across representation levels.

</details>


### [25] [CoGeo-GS: Concept-Driven and Geometry-Aware Multi-Object Removal in 3D Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26656)
*Yuanxiang Ni,Xianliang Huang,Chenhang Ma,Chen Xiao,Yuewen Ma,Ruxin Wang,Hao Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Multi-object removal in 3D scenes is challenging due to severe occlusions, semantic entanglement, and the difficulty of maintaining geometric and multi-view consistency. Existing 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) methods perform well for single-object editing but scale poorly to multi-object scenarios, often requiring repetitive optimization and yielding unstable geometry in removed regions. We propose CoGeo-GS, a concept-driven framework for controllable multi-object removal in 3D scenes. CoGeo-GS assigns concept-aware semantic tags to Gaussians, enabling flexible object selection and reducing interference between foreground objects and background structures within a single optimization stage. To recover plausible geometry, we introduce a geometry-aware completion pipeline that combines monocular depth priors with diffusion-based refinement and boundary-aligned blending. A geometry-regularized refinement strategy further stabilizes reconstruction and preserves multi-view consistency. Experiments demonstrate that CoGeo-GS outperforms existing methods in visual quality and reconstruction fidelity.

</details>


### [26] [PailitaoGR: Latent Think-with-Images for Generative Image Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26658)
*Xiaomeng Fan,Yueran Liu,Shengyu Zhou,Chenghan Fu,Wanxian Guan,Feng Li,Chuan Yu,Jian Xu,Bo Zheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes PailitaoGR, a generative image retrieval method that extends generative retrieval paradigms to image search. It addresses the challenge that real-world query images contain mixed information (search target, auxiliary evidence, and irrelevant content) by designing two mechanisms: target-focused perception to identify and enhance search target regions, and selective auxiliary-evidence utilization to exploit useful auxiliary information. The method achieves "Zooming without Cropping" and "Reading without OCR" capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Generative retrieval has shown strong performance in generating product semantic identifiers (SIDs), but extending this paradigm to image search is challenging because query images contain diverse information - the search target, useful auxiliary evidence, and irrelevant visual content. The model needs to identify and focus on the search target while selectively utilizing auxiliary evidence.

Method: The paper proposes two key mechanisms: (1) Target-focused perception mechanism with a target Enhancer and learning strategy based on on-policy distillation and attention guidance loss to identify and enhance visual tokens of the search target; (2) Selective auxiliary-evidence utilization mechanism with an auxiliary enhancer and in-capacity incremental contrastive distillation strategy to identify and enhance visual tokens of auxiliary evidence. Training and validation sets are sampled from real-world online image-search logs.

Result: The method outperforms existing baselines by an average of 13.8%, validating its effectiveness.

Conclusion: The proposed PailitaoGR method successfully internalizes target-focused perception and selective auxiliary-evidence utilization into a generative retrieval model, enabling "Zooming without Cropping" and "Reading without OCR" capabilities for image retrieval tasks.

Abstract: Generative retrieval has demonstrated strong performance by directly generating product semantic identifiers (SIDs).
  Extending this paradigm to image search, however, is nontrivial because real-world query images contain diverse information, including the search target, useful auxiliary evidence, and irrelevant visual content.
  This requires the model to identify and focus on the search target while selectively utilizing auxiliary evidence. In this paper, we propose \textbf{PailitaoGR}, a \emph{Latent Think-with-Images} method for generative image retrieval, which internalizes target-focused perception and selective auxiliary-evidence utilization into a the generative retrieval model, enabling \textit{Zooming without Cropping} and \textit{Reading without OCR}. Specifically, we design a target-focused perception mechanism that identifies and enhances visual tokens of the search target, consisting of a target Enhancer and a learning strategy based on on-policy distillation and attention guidance loss, enabling the model to focus on search-target regions. We also design a selective auxiliary-evidence utilization mechanism that identifies and enhances visual tokens of auxiliary evidence, including an auxiliary enhancer and an in-capacity incremental contrastive distillation strategy, enabling the model to exploit auxiliary evidence. We construct training and validation sets sampled from real-world online image-search logs. Experiments show that our method outperforms existing baselines by an average of 13.8\%, validating its effectiveness.

</details>


### [27] [Hull First, Wake Second: Wake-Reliance Suppression for Robust Maritime Vessel Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26665)
*Yefan Wang,Xingyu Wang,Ruibiao Zhu,Yusen Wu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HullWake is a hull-first wake-second framework for robust maritime vessel detection that addresses the wake-reliance problem where detectors may miss vessels with weak wakes or produce false positives on wake-like water clutter. It introduces techniques to separate hull evidence from wake context and suppress wake-dominant predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Maritime vessel detectors often face challenging scenes where hulls are small, low-contrast, or blurred, while only wakes are more visible. This creates a "wake-reliance problem": detectors may miss slow or stationary vessels with weak wakes, or produce false positives on wake-like water clutter.

Method: HullWake separates proposal-centered hull evidence from directional wake context, extracts wake cues with bidirectional proposal-anchored corridors, and suppresses wake-dominant predictions through four mechanisms: (1) wake response supervision, (2) wake-attenuated consistency, (3) wake-only confidence suppression, and (4) hull-wake decorrelation. A wake-oriented evaluation protocol is also introduced covering weak/no-wake vessels, wake-like hard negatives, worst-group AP, and confidence drop after wake attenuation.

Result: HullWake improves overall AP, weak/no-wake robustness, wake-like false positives, worst-group AP, and confidence stability after wake attenuation compared to box-only detectors and mask-supervised segmentation baselines.

Conclusion: The hull-first wake-second approach effectively addresses the wake-reliance problem in maritime vessel detection, with the introduced Curated-Wake dataset and wake-oriented evaluation protocol providing valuable benchmarks for future research.

Abstract: Maritime vessel detectors often face scenes where hulls are small, low-contrast, or blurred, while wakes are longer and easier to detect. This creates a wake-reliance problem: detectors may miss slow or stationary vessels with weak wakes, or produce false positives on wake-like water clutter. We propose HullWake, a hull-first wake-second framework for robust maritime vessel detection. HullWake separates proposal-centered hull evidence from directional wake context, extracts wake cues with bidirectional proposal-anchored corridors, and suppresses wake-dominant predictions through wake response supervision, wake-attenuated consistency, wake-only confidence suppression, and hull--wake decorrelation. We also introduce a wake-oriented evaluation protocol covering weak/no-wake vessels, wake-like hard negatives, worst-group AP, and confidence drop after wake attenuation. Experiments are conducted on Curated-Wake, a wake-oriented maritime dataset of about 10,000 images curated from Ships/Vessels in Aerial Images, the SMD benchmark, and SeaDronesSee, with newly added detection- and segmentation-level wake annotations. Compared with box-only detectors and mask-supervised segmentation baselines, HullWake improves overall AP, weak/no-wake robustness, wake-like false positives, worst-group AP, and confidence stability after wake attenuation.

</details>


### [28] [RECAP-Forcing: Retaining Content Appearances for Long Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26671)
*Haiyang Xu,Zheng Ding,Zhuowen Tu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: RECAP-Forcing is a training-free inference method for long autoregressive video generation that organizes memory by appearance novelty rather than recency. It retains KV cache for newly appearing content (entering subjects, disoccluded regions, new scenes) at first visibility, scaling memory with new content rather than video length.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Long autoregressive video generation faces a fundamental memory challenge: with finite attention window, models must decide which information from an ever-expanding history to retain. Existing methods organize memory temporally, preserving recent frames while compressing or discarding older ones, which can lose important information about earlier content.

Method: The framework unifies two mechanisms under the appearance-novelty principle: (1) An attention sink preserves the initial scene at the video beginning when all visible content is novel; (2) An optical-flow-based novelty bank extends this principle by selectively retaining newly revealed content. Memory scales with newly introduced content rather than video length. The method is training-free with no additional learnable parameters.

Result: RECAP-Forcing consistently improves visual quality and semantic fidelity across multiple strong baselines and outperforms existing memory methods.

Conclusion: Appearance-indexed memory makes long-range consistency an explicit property of the memory structure. By prioritizing novelty over recency and scaling memory with new content rather than video length, RECAP-Forcing provides an effective solution for maintaining consistency in long video generation without requiring additional training.

Abstract: Long autoregressive video generation faces a fundamental memory challenge: with a finite attention window, a model must decide which information from an ever-expanding history to retain. Existing methods organize memory temporally, preserving recent frames while compressing or discarding older ones. We instead propose RECAP-Forcing, organizing memory by appearance novelty. A long video is not merely a sequence of frames, but an evolving cast of subjects, objects, and scenes whose identities must remain consistent over time. We organize memory by retaining the KV cache associated with newly appearing content--such as entering subjects, disoccluded regions, and newly introduced scenes--at the moment it first becomes visible, prioritizing novelty over recency. Memory should scale with the amount of newly introduced content, rather than with video length. This appearance-indexed memory makes long-range consistency an explicit property of the memory structure. Our framework unifies two mechanisms under this single principle. At the beginning of a video, when all visible content is novel, an attention sink preserves the initial scene. As the video evolves, an optical-flow-based novelty bank extends the same principle by selectively retaining newly revealed content. As a training-free inference method with no additional learnable parameters, RECAP-Forcing consistently improves visual quality and semantic fidelity across multiple strong baselines and outperforms existing memory methods.

</details>


### [29] [Reason in the Words You Speak: Idiolectal Paraphrasing Off-Policy Traces for Reasoning Distillation in VideoLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26684)
*Ji Soo Lee,Jinyoung Park,Seohyun Lee,Jongha Kim,Joonmyung Choi,Jinsung Yoon,Hyunwoo J. Kim*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Echo-GRPO addresses GRPO's limitation of being bounded to a model's existing reasoning capabilities by rewriting privileged teacher reasoning traces into the student model's own characteristic language patterns (idiolect), while preserving semantics via Dual-Reference Decoding. Applied as VideoEcho-R1 for video reasoning distillation, it consistently improves three multimodal LLM backbones across five benchmarks and also benefits supervised fine-tuning frameworks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is on-policy and thus limits the model to reasoning skills it can already produce. Prior works use privileged reasoning traces from stronger teacher models, but these are out-of-distribution relative to the student policy, causing gradient clipping on critical reasoning tokens and leaving the underlying reasoning unlearned despite rewarding correct answers.

Method: Echo-GRPO rewrites teacher reasoning traces into the student policy's idiolect (characteristic vocabulary and expression patterns) while preserving semantics via Dual-Reference Decoding. This creates policy-aligned supervision that the student can actually learn from. The approach is instantiated as VideoEcho-R1 for video reasoning distillation and can be used as a plug-in module for both RL and SFT frameworks.

Result: VideoEcho-R1 achieves consistent improvements across three multimodal LLM backbones and five benchmarks. The idiolectal paraphrasing module consistently improves both GRPO-based RL and supervised fine-tuning frameworks for reasoning distillation.

Conclusion: Policy-aligned supervision (rewriting traces into the student's own language patterns) is more effective than direct imitation of out-of-distribution teacher traces. This approach extends beyond GRPO to also benefit SFT frameworks, demonstrating that matching the teacher's reasoning style to the student's linguistic characteristics is a general principle for effective reasoning distillation.

Abstract: Recent large language models achieve strong performance on complex reasoning tasks, where reinforcement learning with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) has emerged as a leading paradigm for optimizing models on self-generated trajectories. However, the on-policy nature of GRPO bounds the model to the reasoning skills it can already produce, restricting to learn more advanced capabilities. Prior works inject privileged reasoning traces from a stronger teacher policy to guide training, yet these traces are inherently out of distribution with respect to the student policy. We observe that this mismatch between on-policy and off-policy causes gradient clipping on semantically critical reasoning tokens, ultimately rewarding correct answers while leaving the reasoning that justifies them unlearned. Hence, we propose \textbf{Echo-GRPO}, a framework that lets the model reason in the words it speaks. Rather than imitating low-probability privileged traces from the teacher model, Echo-GRPO rewrites them into the student policy's own \textit{idiolect}, that is, its own characteristic vocabulary and expression patterns, while preserving their semantics via Dual-Reference Decoding. We instantiate this framework as \textbf{VideoEcho-R1} for video reasoning distillation, achieving consistent improvements across three multimodal LLM backbones and five benchmarks. Finally, we show that our idiolectal paraphrasing is a plug-in module that consistently improves both RL and supervised fine-tuning frameworks for reasoning distillation, demonstrating that policy-aligned supervision extends beyond GRPO.

</details>


### [30] [Domain-Specific Self-Supervised Representation Learning for Retinal Fundus Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26686)
*Bekzat Nurlanbekova,Fung Fung Ting*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper investigates contrastive self-supervised learning (SSL) methods (SimSiam and SimCLR) for retinal disease classification from fundus images, addressing the scarcity of annotated medical data through representation learning from unlabeled images.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Annotated medical images are scarce and costly to obtain. Supervised learning requires large labeled datasets, which are impractical in the medical domain. This work aims to reduce dependence on large annotated datasets by leveraging self-supervised learning to extract useful representations from unlabeled fundus images.

Method: The authors employ two SSL frameworks (SimSiam and SimCLR) with retinal-specific augmentation techniques, training with small batch sizes under resource-constrained settings. Representation quality is assessed via linear evaluation and fine-tuning on downstream tasks: multi-disease classification and diabetic retinopathy grading.

Result: Tailoring augmentation strategies to retinal image characteristics significantly improves performance. Even with limited data and computational resources, lightweight SSL frameworks can learn transferable representations that achieve competitive results compared to supervised methods.

Conclusion: Self-supervised learning is a viable approach to reduce annotation burden in medical imaging. Custom augmentation strategies specific to the target domain (retinal images) are critical for learning high-quality representations, enabling effective transfer learning for downstream clinical tasks.

Abstract: Despite the growing number of public datasets, annotated medical images remain scarce. Supervised learning methods achieve strong performance on many benchmarks, however require large amounts of labeled data, which are costly and time-consuming to obtain in the medical domain. To address this limitation, contrastive self-supervised learning (SSL) has emerged as a promising alternative for learning useful representations from unlabeled data. In this work, we investigate two SSL frameworks, SimSiam and SimCLR, for retinal disease classification from fundus images. We focus on understanding how augmentation strategies and training parameters influence representation learning under resource-constrained settings. Given limited data and computational capacity, we explore the feasibility of training SSL models with small batch sizes incorporated with retinal-specific augmentation techniques. Through a series of experiments, we assess the quality of learned representations via linear evaluation and fine-tuning across downstream tasks, including multi-disease classification and diabetic retinopathy grading. Our results show that tailoring augmentation strategies to the characteristics of retinal images plays a critical role in improving performance. Even under constrained settings, lightweight SSL frameworks can learn transferable representations that reduce dependence on large annotated datasets and achieve competitive results.

</details>


### [31] [AesCanvas: A Large-Scale Dataset and Benchmark for Aesthetic Critique and Contextual Suitability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26713)
*Xuanwei Hu,Haoyu Dong,Kejun Wu,Tianyi Liu,Jianjun Gao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AesCanvas introduces a unified benchmark suite for evaluating MLLMs on contextual aesthetic assessment, comprising CritiqueCanvas (519,136 instruction-response pairs for multi-dimensional critique) and ContextCanvas (301 expert-reviewed use scenarios for contextual suitability). The study reveals a gap between critique generation capability and context-sensitive judgment in current MLLMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing IAA benchmarks only assess intrinsic visual quality or fixed domain criteria, ignoring whether an image is appropriate for specific purposes, audiences, cultural settings, or domain conventions. There is a need to evaluate contextual aesthetic suitability - whether an appealing image is actually suitable for its intended use.

Method: The authors created AesCanvas with two components: (1) CritiqueCanvas with 519,136 instruction-response pairs from 54,300 images supporting long-form, multi-dimensional critique across photography, painting, and virtual imagery; (2) ContextCanvas with 301 expert-reviewed use scenarios evaluating contextual aesthetic suitability. They evaluated closed-source frontier, open-weight general, and aesthetic-specific MLLMs under a unified protocol.

Result: Results show clear separation between critique generation and context-sensitive judgment: reference-based lexical and semantic metrics only partially capture critique quality; aesthetic specialists remain competitive on selected critique metrics but substantially lag strong general-purpose MLLMs on ContextCanvas. Aesthetic specialization does not reliably transfer to contextual suitability, and model decisions may fail to track or ground themselves in decisive contextual visual cues.

Conclusion: Culturally situated, evidence-grounded suitability is a distinct objective for aesthetic modeling. The study establishes that contextual aesthetic suitability is a separate capability from critique generation, requiring different evaluation metrics and model architectures.

Abstract: Recent advances in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have extended Image Aesthetic Assessment (IAA) beyond scalar scores toward interpretable critique and guidance. Yet existing benchmarks mainly assess intrinsic visual quality or fixed domain criteria, leaving open whether an appealing image is appropriate for a specific purpose, audience, cultural setting, or domain convention. We introduce AesCanvas, a unified suite with two complementary components: CritiqueCanvas with 519,136 instruction-response pairs from 54,300 images supports long-form, multi-dimensional critique across photography, painting, and virtual imagery, whereas ContextCanvas with 301 expert-reviewed use scenarios evaluates contextual aesthetic suitability in realistic use scenarios. Under a unified protocol, we evaluate closed-source frontier, open-weight general, and aesthetic-specific MLLMs. Results reveal a clear separation between critique generation and context-sensitive judgment: reference-based lexical and semantic metrics only partially capture critique quality, while aesthetic specialists remain competitive on selected critique metrics yet substantially lag strong general-purpose MLLMs on ContextCanvas. Further analyses show that aesthetic specialization does not reliably transfer to contextual suitability and that model decisions may fail to track or ground themselves in decisive contextual visual cues. These findings establish culturally situated, evidence-grounded suitability as a distinct objective for aesthetic modeling.

</details>


### [32] [LiveVVT: High-Fidelity Video Virtual Try-On in Real Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26714)
*Yushe Cao,Shikun Feng,Ruxiang Duan,Liyong Wang,Dianxi Shi,Chun Yu,Junliang Xing*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LiveVVT introduces a rolling streaming diffusion framework for video virtual try-on that achieves real-time performance by preserving bounded bidirectional modeling within causal recurrent generation, using dual memory mechanisms (temporal and appearance) and a progressive distillation training approach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Diffusion-based Video Virtual Try-On (VVT) achieves high visual fidelity through bidirectional spatio-temporal modeling, but complete-clip dependence causes prohibitive latency and computational overhead in practical continuous deployment. Naively enforcing causality disrupts pretrained bidirectional priors and substantially degrades synthesis quality.

Method: LiveVVT uses a fixed-size window to jointly denoise multiple video chunks under bounded look-ahead, preserving local bidirectional interactions while emitting one clean chunk per iteration. Two complementary memories sustain long-term consistency: (1) bounded temporal memory propagates recent dynamics and occlusion context, and (2) persistent global appearance memory anchors garment details throughout the stream. Training uses a progressive distillation framework integrating bidirectional VVT learning, teacher-trajectory regression for causal few-step adaptation, and Collaborative Matching Distillation.

Result: Experiments on paired and unpaired long-sequence benchmarks demonstrate superior generation quality over similarly sized models, with 26× lower latency and 11× higher throughput.

Conclusion: LiveVVT enables high-fidelity real-time streaming VVT by balancing bidirectional modeling with causal generation through bounded window denoising and dual memory mechanisms, achieving significant efficiency gains while maintaining quality.

Abstract: Diffusion-based Video Virtual Try-On (VVT) achieves high visual fidelity through bidirectional spatio-temporal modeling, but complete-clip dependence incurs prohibitive latency and computational overhead in practical continuous deployment. Naively enforcing causality disrupts pretrained bidirectional priors and substantially degrades synthesis quality. We introduce LiveVVT, a rolling streaming diffusion framework that preserves bounded bidirectional modeling within causal recurrent generation. Within a fixed-size window, LiveVVT jointly denoises multiple video chunks under bounded look-ahead, preserving local bidirectional interactions while emitting one clean chunk per iteration. Beyond the window, two complementary memories sustain long-term consistency: a bounded temporal memory propagates recent dynamics and occlusion context, whereas a persistent global appearance memory, constructed once from the target garment and a frontal try-on keyframe, anchors garment details and dressed appearance throughout the stream. We further introduce a progressive distillation framework integrating bidirectional VVT learning, teacher-trajectory regression for causal few-step adaptation, and Collaborative Matching Distillation, which couples teacher-distribution matching with rolling flow matching on real videos to align optimization with recurrent inference. Experiments on paired and unpaired long-sequence benchmarks demonstrate superior generation quality over similarly sized models, with $26\times$ lower latency and $11\times$ higher throughput, enabling high-fidelity real-time streaming VVT.

</details>


### [33] [Beyond Atomic Layouts: Compositional Design Understanding with Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26716)
*Yiyang Huang,Zhaowen Wang,Simon Jenni,Jing Shi,Yitian Zhang,Yizhou Wang,Yun Fu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper introduces compositional layout understanding as a new task, addressing the challenge of interpreting visually entangled elements in hierarchical multi-layer structures. The authors present CoDeLayout (~20K annotated real-world layouts) and MASON, a post-training paradigm combining multimodal alignment and structural perception to overcome semantic drift and structural ambiguity in existing VLMs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing vision-language models excel at atomic layouts but struggle with compositional layouts requiring reasoning over visually entangled elements within hierarchical multi-layer structures. Two key challenges were identified: semantic drift between textual metadata and visual content, and structural ambiguity in hierarchical inter-element relationships.

Method: The authors propose MASON, a post-training paradigm with two components: (1) Multimodal Alignment (MA) - enhances element interpretation by grounding metadata-defined elements to their visual counterparts, mitigating semantic drift; (2) Structural Perception (SP) - models layer-aware inter-element spatial relationships to improve hierarchical understanding and reduce structural ambiguity.

Result: Empirical evaluation on CoDeLayout reveals substantial gaps in existing VLMs. The strongest baseline, GPT-o3, achieves only 79.68% accuracy. Qwen2.5-VL 7B with MASON reaches 91.66% accuracy. Notably, MASON surpasses full-data Direct Finetune using only 30% of the training data and scales better with additional data.

Conclusion: The study successfully establishes compositional layout understanding as a meaningful research direction. MASON demonstrates that combining multimodal alignment with structural perception effectively addresses the identified challenges, achieving state-of-the-art performance while requiring less training data than conventional fine-tuning approaches.

Abstract: Layout understanding, or the interpretation of element organization, is essential for document analysis, user interface (UI) creation, and graphic design. While recent vision-language models (VLMs) excel at interpreting atomic layouts composed of independent elements, they struggle with compositional layouts that require reasoning over visually entangled elements within hierarchical multi-layer structures. In this paper, we introduce a new task, compositional layout understanding, and present CoDeLayout, a VQA dataset of ~20K real-world multi-layer layouts annotated with compositional element pairs and design intent. Through empirical analysis on CoDeLayout, we identify two key challenges for existing VLMs: semantic drift between textual metadata and visual content, and structural ambiguity in hierarchical inter-element relationships. To address these challenges, we propose MASON, a post-training paradigm that integrates multimodal alignment (MA) and structural perception (SP). MA enhances element interpretation by grounding metadata-defined elements to their visual counterparts, mitigating semantic drift, while SP models layer-aware inter-element spatial relationships to improve hierarchical understanding and reduce structural ambiguity. Experiments reveal substantial gaps in existing VLMs: even the strongest baseline, GPT-o3, achieves only 79.68% accuracy, whereas Qwen2.5-VL 7B with MASON reaches 91.66%. Notably, MASON surpasses full-data Direct Finetune using only 30% of the training data and scales better with additional data.

</details>


### [34] [Parameter Efficient Continual Learning for Sparse Event-Based Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26720)
*Vaishnavi Nagabhushana,Kartikay Agrawal,Ayon Borthakur*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: sLoTh is a parameter-efficient continual learning framework for sparse event-based vision transformers that enables adaptation by updating less than 1% of model parameters without replay buffers, achieving 6.5x lower energy consumption than dense vision transformers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robotic and edge intelligence systems require models to adapt to continuously arriving data while preserving previously learned knowledge under strict memory and energy constraints. While parameter-efficient fine-tuning shows promise for continual learning with vision transformers, conventional dense architectures remain costly for real-world deployment. The continual learning capabilities of sparse event-based vision transformers remain largely unexplored.

Method: sLoTh freezes the backbone and restricts plasticity to two components: (1) scalable-efficient low-rank attention updates (seLoRA) and (2) shared neuronal threshold modulation. This enables parameter-efficient adaptation without requiring replay buffers.

Result: Experiments across CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet, ImageNet-100, and ImageNet-R with up to 100 tasks demonstrate competitive rehearsal-free performance in class-incremental learning and online continual learning, while achieving approximately 6.5x lower energy consumption than conventional dense vision transformers.

Conclusion: sLoTh successfully enables efficient continual learning for sparse event-based vision transformers, making them viable for energy-constrained edge and robotic applications while maintaining strong performance without replay-based memory storage.

Abstract: Robotic and edge intelligence systems operate in dynamic environments where data arrives continuously, requiring models to adapt while preserving previously learned knowledge under strict memory and energy constraints. While parameter-efficient fine-tuning has shown promise for continual learning with vision transformers, conventional architectures rely on dense computation and remain costly for real-world deployment. Sparse event-based vision transformers provide energy-efficient event-driven computation, yet their continual learning capabilities remain largely unexplored. We here introduce sLoTh, a parameter-efficient continual learning framework for pretrained sparse event-based (spiking) vision transformers. sLoTh freezes the backbone and restricts plasticity to scalable-efficient low-rank attention updates (seLoRA) and shared neuronal threshold modulation, enabling adaptation without replay buffers by updating less than 1% of model parameters. Experiments across CIFAR-100, Tiny-ImageNet, ImageNet-100, and ImageNet-R with up to 100 tasks demonstrate competitive rehearsal-free performance in class-incremental learning and online continual learning, while enabling approximately 6.5x lower energy consumption than conventional dense vision transformers.

</details>


### [35] [UniGeo: A Multi-modal Large Language Model for Text-Guided Cross-View Geo-Localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26722)
*Jiahao Wen,Hang Yu,Zhedong Zheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: UniGeo is a unified multimodal large language model (MLLM) for text-guided drone geo-localization that jointly supports geo-semantic understanding, cross-view semantic generation, and candidate-level verification through a multi-stage training strategy, achieving significant improvements on GeoText-1652 dataset.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing methods formulate text-guided drone geo-localization as direct matching between text queries and candidate images, but this approach is insufficient due to incomplete queries and highly similar candidates, which make global cross-modal matching unreliable for fine-grained localization.

Method: UniGeo is built on a shared vision-language framework and employs three key components: (1) geo-semantic learning to establish stable correspondences among local scene elements, spatial relations, and language descriptions; (2) cross-view semantic generation to model semantic mappings between drone and satellite views; (3) a plug-and-play verification module for fine-grained discrimination among confusable candidates. A multi-stage training strategy progressively learns geo-semantic understanding, cross-view generation, and candidate verification.

Result: On GeoText-1652, UniGeo improves R@10 by 13.59 percentage points and mAP by 2.83 percentage points, demonstrating consistent improvements across multiple retrieval backbones.

Conclusion: UniGeo effectively addresses fine-grained text-guided drone geo-localization through unified multimodal learning with geo-semantic understanding, cross-view generation, and candidate verification capabilities, validating its effectiveness with significant performance gains.

Abstract: Text-guided drone geo-localization aims to identify a target region in a large-scale image gallery from a natural-language description. Existing methods mainly formulate this task as direct matching between an open-ended text query and candidate images. However, incomplete queries and highly similar candidates often make global cross-modal matching insufficient for reliable fine-grained localization. We propose UniGeo, a unified multimodal large language model (MLLM) for text-guided drone geo-localization. Built on a shared vision-language framework, UniGeo jointly supports geo-semantic understanding, cross-view semantic generation, and candidate-level verification. Specifically, it establishes stable correspondences among local scene elements, spatial relations, and language descriptions through geo-semantic learning, and further models semantic mappings between drone and satellite views through cross-view generation. Based on these capabilities, a plug-and-play verification module performs fine-grained discrimination among highly confusable candidates. We further introduce a multi-stage training strategy that progressively learns geo-semantic understanding, cross-view generation, and candidate verification, improving adaptation to text-guided geo-localization. Experiments demonstrate consistent improvements across multiple retrieval backbones. On GeoText-1652, UniGeo improves R@10 and mAP by 13.59 and 2.83 percentage points, respectively, validating its effectiveness for fine-grained text-guided drone geo-localization.

</details>


### [36] [GeoMAD: Geometry-Aware Multi-View Anomaly Detection via Deformable Fusion and Distributional Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26724)
*Shang-Fu Chen,Jhih-Ciang Wu,Kuan-Chuan Peng,Wen-Huang Cheng,Kai-Lung Hua*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GeoMAD is a unified multi-view, multi-class industrial anomaly detection framework that addresses geometric correspondence deficiency and distributional inconsistency through two novel modules: Cross-view Deformable Fusion Module (CDFM) for hierarchical cross-view correspondence without 3D supervision, and Distributional View Alignment (DVA) for global consistency regularization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing multi-view anomaly detection methods suffer from a trade-off: voxel-based fusion provides geometric alignment but requires costly 3D construction and class-specific assumptions, while patch-based fusion is efficient but lacks continuous cross-view correspondence and relies on discrete candidate matching.

Method: The paper proposes two key innovations: (1) Cross-view Deformable Fusion Module (CDFM) that learns content-adaptive, view-pair-specific sampling offsets on 2D feature maps arranged in a multi-scale window pyramid with image-global reference, enabling hierarchical cross-view correspondence without camera calibration, voxel construction, or class-specific 3D supervision; (2) Distributional View Alignment (DVA), a self-supervised cross-view regularization loss that aligns each view's bottleneck distribution against a per-instance view-centric target for global consistency.

Result: GeoMAD achieves strong detection and localization performance on Real-IAD and MANTA-Tiny benchmarks in unified multi-view multi-class anomaly detection settings.

Conclusion: The proposed CDFM and DVA effectively bridge local geometric correspondence and global distributional consistency, providing geometry-aware and distribution-consistent fusion while preserving the efficiency of 2D feature-space learning.

Abstract: Multi-view anomaly detection (MvAD) detects defects by exploiting complementary observations from multiple camera viewpoints. The central challenge is to fuse views with sufficient geometric awareness while remaining scalable to multi-class industrial settings. Existing methods typically fall into two extremes: voxel-based fusion provides explicit geometric alignment but requires costly 3D construction and class-specific assumptions, whereas lightweight patch-based fusion is efficient but relies on discrete candidate matching and lacks continuous cross-view correspondence. In this paper, we propose GeoMAD, a unified multi-view, multi-class AD framework that addresses both geometric correspondence deficiency and distributional inconsistency. Our \textit{Cross-view Deformable Fusion Module} (CDFM) learns content-adaptive, view-pair-specific sampling offsets directly on 2D feature maps and arranges them across a multi-scale window pyramid with image-global reference sampling, enabling hierarchical cross-view correspondence without camera calibration, voxel construction, or class-specific 3D supervision. We further introduce \textit{Distributional View Alignment} (DVA), a self-supervised cross-view regularization loss that aligns each view's bottleneck distribution against a per-instance view-centric target, enforcing global consistency without pixel-level correspondence. Together, CDFM and DVA bridge local geometric correspondence and global distributional consistency, providing geometry-aware and distribution-consistent fusion while preserving the efficiency of 2D feature-space learning. Extensive experiments on Real-IAD and MANTA-Tiny show that GeoMAD achieves strong detection and localization performance in unified MvAD.

</details>


### [37] [Generative Semantic Scene Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26737)
*Shi Chen,Weifeng Ge*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper presents Generative Semantic Scene Completion (GSSC), a novel framework that uses discrete diffusion to recover dense 3D semantic voxel grids from sparse LiDAR observations (observing only 1% of the target volume). The approach addresses extreme class imbalance (7,000x) through three components: paired sparse-dense scene synthesis (PS³) for data generation, semantic-guided generative completion (SGSC) for generation from noise, and structured source discrete diffusion (S²D²) for refinement. The method achieves 38.8% mIoU on SemanticKITTI hidden test (best causal single-sweep single-sample result) and 39.2% with additional augmentation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Outdoor LiDAR semantic scene completion faces extreme challenges: sparse observations covering only 1% of the target volume and severe class imbalance exceeding 7,000x ratio between majority and minority classes. Existing methods struggle with the long-tail distribution problem at its source.

Method: The paper proposes a single discrete-diffusion formulation with three roles: (1) PS³ generates matched sparse LiDAR observations with dense semantic completions to address the long tail at its source, creating the PS³-SemanticKITTI corpus; (2) SGSC generates scenes from noise using multinomial discrete diffusion, conditioned on sparse scans through BEV semantic maps and sparse 3D feature streams; (3) S²D² refines existing completions in one flow-matching step without retraining or test-time adaptation.

Result: S²D² improves mIoU of SGSC's own output and every external SSC base tested. The strongest base achieves 38.8% mIoU on SemanticKITTI hidden test (best causal single-sweep single-sample result), +2.1 pp over previous best. With four correction steps and eight-view TTA, it reaches 39.2%.

Conclusion: The GSSC framework successfully addresses sparse LiDAR semantic completion through discrete diffusion, achieving state-of-the-art results on SemanticKITTI. The key innovation is treating SSC as a generative task, addressing class imbalance at the data source and enabling flexible refinement through the same diffusion framework. S²D² provides universal improvement across all base methods without retraining.

Abstract: Outdoor LiDAR semantic scene completion (SSC) recovers a dense semantic voxel grid from a scan observing 1% of the target volume, under class imbalance beyond 7,000x. We recast SSC as generative semantic scene completion (GSSC): a single discrete-diffusion formulation in three roles. First, paired sparse-dense scene synthesis (PS$^3$) generates matched sparse LiDAR observations with their dense semantic completions, addressing the long tail at its source and yielding the PS$^3$-SemanticKITTI corpus we train on alongside SemanticKITTI. Second, semantic-guided generative scene completion (SGSC) generates the scene from noise with multinomial discrete diffusion, conditioned on the sparse scan through a bird's-eye-view semantic map and a sparse 3D feature stream. Third, the same framework instead refines an existing completion in one flow-matching step: structured source discrete diffusion (S$^2$D$^2$). S$^2$D$^2$ improves the mIoU of SGSC's own output and every external SSC base tested, without base retraining or test-time adaptation. On the strongest base, one step without test-time augmentation reaches 38.8% mIoU on the SemanticKITTI hidden test. To our knowledge that is the best causal, single-sweep, single-sample result on that leaderboard, +2.1 pp over the previous best published score under the same restriction. Four correction steps with eight-view test-time augmentation reach 39.2%, outside that restriction.

</details>


### [38] [G2D: Generative-to-Discriminative Collaborative Inference for Zero-Shot Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26744)
*Zehua Hao,Fang Liu,Qinliang Wang,Yaoyang Du,Xinyan Huang,Puhua Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Zero-shot classification needs efficient label retrieval and fine-grained visual reasoning, yet discriminative and generative vision-language models fail in complementary ways.When CLIP's top-1 prediction is wrong, the correct label often remains in its top-$K$ shortlist, making disambiguation rather than recall the key challenge.Standalone generative models, however, are hindered by large label spaces and unconstrained outputs.This complementarity motivates separating broad candidate retrieval from fine-grained, image-grounded verification.We propose G2D, a training-free framework that uses a generative VLM to verify CLIP-retrieved candidates against the image.Candidate names and CLIP probabilities provide a structured prior for resolving visually similar classes.Fixed confidence routing, entropy-adaptive candidate sizing, and trie-constrained decoding focus generative reasoning on uncertain samples and ensure one valid output for each input at test time.Across eight benchmarks, G2D achieves 68.85% average accuracy, versus 59.35% for CLIP and 63.11% for the standalone VLM.Across seven generator configurations, candidate-set verification improves average accuracy by 1.08--27.42 percentage points.G2D also transfers to DCLIP, WaffleCLIP, and CuPL, supporting a practical interface between discriminative proposal and generative visual reasoning. Code: https://github.com/Harzva/G2D

</details>


### [39] [Glass Surface Detection Grounded in 3D Visual Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26752)
*Yiwei Lu,Ke Xu,Tao Yan,Xiaojun Chang,Radu Timofte,Rynson W. H. Lau*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel 3D geometry-grounded approach for glass surface detection (GSD), shifting from traditional 2D appearance-based methods to explicitly modeling the physical existence of glass surfaces using 3D visual geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Glass surface detection is critical for scene understanding and reconstruction but remains challenging due to glass transparency and reflectivity. Existing methods rely on 2D appearance cues, which fail in geometrically ambiguous scenes.

Method: The method distills rich 3D priors from VGGT (visual geometry grounded transformer) to generate glass-aware 3D representations. It employs multi-tasking learning with a novel glass detection head containing two core modules: (1) Frequency Self-Attention Module (FSAM) for identifying glass-specific spectral features for localization, and (2) Geometry Grounding Block (GeGB) for selectively grounding 2D features in 3D geometry for segmentation.

Result: The method achieves state-of-the-art performance across seven standard GSD benchmarks, generalizes well to video/multi-modal data, and substantially improves reconstruction in glass scenes.

Conclusion: Grounding GSD in 3D visual geometry represents a paradigm shift that enables more robust and accurate glass surface detection compared to traditional 2D appearance-based approaches.

Abstract: Glass surface detection (GSD) is critical for scene understanding and reconstruction, and yet remains challenging due to the transparency and reflectivity of glass surfaces. Existing GSD methods typically rely on 2D appearance cues, which may fail in geometrically ambiguous scenes. In this paper, we propose a paradigm shift: grounding GSD in 3D visual geometry to explicitly model the physical existence of glass surfaces. Our method first distills rich 3D priors from the visual geometry grounded transformer (VGGT) and generates glass-aware 3D representations. It then exploits multi-tasking learning with a novel glass detection head, consisting of two core modules: a Frequency Self-Attention Module (FSAM) that identifies glass-specific spectral features for glass surface localization, and a Geometry Grounding Block (GeGB) that selectively grounds 2D features in 3D geometry for glass surface segmentation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance across seven standard GSD benchmarks, generalizes well to video/multi-modal data, and substantially improves reconstruction in glass scenes. Code is available in https://github.com/YT3DVision/VGGT_GLASS.

</details>


### [40] [Cross-Architecture Knowledge Distillation from a Vision Foundation Model to a Lightweight Visual State Space Model for Tea Leaf Disease Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26771)
*Zibo Zhou,Zongsen Qiu,Rui Chen,Yujie Yao,Yue Zhou,Jianjun Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper proposes cross-architecture knowledge distillation from a fine-tuned DINOv2 teacher (22M params) to a compact Visual State Space Model student (4.45M params) for tea leaf disease classification on edge devices. The authors fix training stability issues with a progressive convolutional stem and gated bidirectional selective-scan block, achieving 95.41% accuracy (+3.09 pp over baseline) while using 5x fewer parameters and retaining 98.3% of teacher accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deploying accurate disease classification models on edge devices in precision agriculture is challenging due to tight compute budgets. Self-supervised vision foundation models like DINOv2 are too large, while lightweight models trained from scratch on small agricultural datasets underfit.

Method: Cross-architecture knowledge distillation from Vision Transformer (DINOv2) teacher to Visual State Space Model (LVSSM) student. Fixed two training stability problems: (1) replaced single large patch-embedding convolution with progressive convolutional stem, (2) added gated bidirectional selective-scan block to restore residual path. Used temperature-scaled logit distillation with simple logit-level KD (intermediate feature-alignment losses were found to reduce accuracy).

Result: Temperature-scaled logit distillation raised test accuracy from 92.32+/-2.14% to 95.41+/-1.17% across three seeds (+3.09 percentage-point mean gain). Best single run: 96.20%; macro-F1: 94.45%. Student uses 5.0 times fewer parameters than the 22M-parameter teacher while retaining 98.3% of its accuracy.

Conclusion: Cross-architecture KD from ViT teacher to SSM student is feasible and effective for edge-deployable tea leaf disease classification. Simple logit-level KD outperforms feature-alignment losses. Limitations include single-dataset scope and simplified non-official SSM implementation.

Abstract: Automated tea leaf disease classification supports precision agriculture, yet deploying accurate models on edge devices remains challenging under tight compute budgets. Self-supervised vision foundation models such as DINOv2 provide strong features but are too large for field deployment, while lightweight models trained from scratch on small agricultural datasets often underfit. We study cross-architecture knowledge distillation (KD) from a fine-tuned DINOv2 teacher (Vision Transformer) to a compact bidirectional Visual State Space Model (LVSSM) student, an underexplored direction because the architectures use fundamentally different token-mixing mechanisms. We identify and fix two training-stability problems that prevent the from-scratch SSM student from learning on limited data: a single large patch-embedding convolution and a fusion layer that severs the residual path. With a progressive convolutional stem and gated bidirectional selective-scan block, the 4.45M-parameter student trains stably. Across three seeds, temperature-scaled logit distillation raises test accuracy from 92.32+/-2.14% to 95.41+/-1.17% (best single run: 96.20%; macro-F1: 94.45%), a +3.09 percentage-point mean gain. The student uses 5.0 times fewer parameters than the 22M-parameter teacher while retaining 98.3% of its accuracy. Ablations show that intermediate feature-alignment losses reduce accuracy, making simple logit-level KD the strongest configuration. A fair from-scratch comparison shows the gain is specific to students that start below the teacher. We report per-class metrics, confusion matrices, bootstrap confidence intervals, and FLOPs/latency measurements, and discuss limitations including the single-dataset scope and simplified non-official SSM implementation.

</details>


### [41] [Ring Forcing: Towards Precise Long-Term Memory for Autoregressive Video Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26794)
*Bowen Xue,Brandon Y. Feng,Chenguo Lin,Yuchen Lin,Yujia Zeng,Lvmin Zhang,Maneesh Agrawala,Honglei Yan,Panwang Pan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Ring Forcing addresses the critical bottleneck of long-term memory in video generation models by introducing a ring-structured training strategy, compression/timestep composition for extended memory capacity, and sparse RoPE for flexible memory adaptation. It achieves superior minutes-long coherence and object permanence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current video generation models lack robust long-term memory, which is essential for scaling to long durations. Two critical aspects are object permanence (reproducing object appearance upon re-entry) and memory capacity (processing ultra-long context). Existing approaches trade off between strict historical adherence and generative diversity.

Method: 1) Ring-structured training strategy: enforces retrieval from distant history to reconcile the trade-off between historical adherence and generative diversity. 2) Compression and timestep composition strategy: extends effective historical span to minutes-long durations under fixed sequence length constraints. 3) Sparse RoPE mechanism: enables flexible, scalable memory adaptation while exploiting pre-trained priors.

Result: Achieves superior minutes-long coherence and object permanence, significantly outperforming state-of-the-art methods.

Conclusion: Ring Forcing presents a novel autoregressive video diffusion framework that successfully addresses both object permanence and memory capacity challenges in long-duration video generation through innovative training and memory mechanisms.

Abstract: Scaling video generation to long durations reveals a critical bottleneck: current models lack robust long-term memory. This deficiency can be studied along two critical aspects: object permanence, the ability to precisely reproduce the appearance of objects upon re-entry; and memory capacity, the ability to process ultra-long context and use information from distant history. Robust long-term memory requires both: object permanence without sufficient context handling limits the temporal scope, while long context length without permanence fails to maintain identity. To address this, we present Ring Forcing, an autoregressive video diffusion framework designed to robustly construct and precisely utilize long-term memory. Our ring-structured training strategy enforces retrieval from distant history, effectively reconciling the trade-off between strict historical adherence and generative diversity. To expand memory capacity, we introduce a compression and timestep composition strategy. Under fixed sequence length constraints, this method extends the effective historical span to minutes-long durations and achieves a comprehensive receptive field over the entire history. Furthermore, we present a sparse RoPE mechanism to enable flexible, scalable memory adaptation while fully exploiting pre-trained priors. Extensive experiments demonstrate that Ring Forcing achieves superior minutes-long coherence and object permanence, significantly outperforming state-of-the-art methods.

</details>


### [42] [Multi-Image Visual Token Pruning in Large Visual Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26806)
*Rongyang Zhang,Chengqiang Lu,Cong Li,Hongchao Gu,Tingjia Shen,Xuyang Zhi,Qimeng Wang,Yan Gao,Yi Wu,Yao Hu,Hao Wang,Enhong Chen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A training-free Adaptive Visual Token Pruning (AVTP) framework for Large Vision Language Models that addresses limitations of static pruning strategies by determining pruning layers based on visual attention distribution analysis and implementing adaptive pruning ratios for multi-image scenarios.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visual token pruning methods rely on static strategies that struggle to adapt across different LVLM architectures and multi-image scenarios, and are constrained by dependence on attention computations incompatible with efficient techniques like FlashAttention.

Method: The framework strategically determines pruning layers based on empirical analysis of visual attention distributions across various LVLMs, and implements adaptive pruning ratios in multi-image contexts where images of higher importance retain proportionally more tokens.

Result: Qwen3VL-8B achieves 2x inference speedup while maintaining 96.1% of original accuracy on multiple multi-image benchmarks; InternVL3.5-8B retains 94.1% accuracy; LLaVA-OV-7B exceeds its original baseline performance.

Conclusion: AVTP demonstrates effectiveness and robustness across different LVLMs as a training-free solution that adapts to diverse architectures and multi-image scenarios while maintaining high accuracy with significant inference speedup.

Abstract: With the growing demand for processing multiple image sequences in real-world applications, various visual token pruning methods have emerged to mitigate the computational and context length constraints faced by Large Vision Language Models (LVLMs). However, most existing pruning approaches rely on static strategies that struggle to adapt across different architectural LVLMs and multi-image scenarios, and are additionally constrained by their dependence on attention computations that are incompatible with efficient techniques like FlashAttention. To address these limitations, we propose a training-free, Adaptive Visual Token Pruning (AVTP) framework, applicable to diverse LVLM architectures. We strategically determine pruning layers based on empirical analysis of visual attention distributions across various LVLMs, and implement adaptive pruning ratios in multi-image contexts where images of higher importance retain proportionally more tokens. We conduct extensive experiments across different LVLMs to demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of AVTP. Specifically, Qwen3VL-8B achieves 2 times inference speedup while maintaining 96.1\% of its original accuracy on multiple multi-image benchmarks, InternVL3.5-8B retains 94.1\% accuracy, and LLaVA-OV-7B even exceeds its original baseline performance. Our code is available at \href{https://github.com/zry13/AVTP}{this link}.

</details>


### [43] [Hyperspectral Diffusion Equivariant Imaging (HyDiff-EI): A Self-supervised Framework for Hyperspectral Image Inpainting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26812)
*Shuo Li,Mike Davies,Mehrdad Yaghoobi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: HyDiff-EI is a novel test-time optimization framework for hyperspectral image inpainting that learns directly from a single corrupted acquisition without requiring large-scale pretraining. It embeds equivariant consistency constraints within the diffusion process to address the ill-posed nature of unsupervised inpainting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional diffusion-based methods rely on large-scale pretraining, which is impractical for remote sensing scenarios where large annotated hyperspectral datasets are limited. The goal is to develop a flexible framework suited for different sensor configurations that can work with single corrupted HSI acquisitions.

Method: HyDiff-EI is a test-time optimization framework that embeds equivariant consistency constraints within the diffusion process. It leverages the inherent geometric symmetries and intrinsic characteristics of HSIs to bridge generative diffusion modeling with self-consistent physical priors.

Result: Coupling diffusion modeling with equivariant priors substantially enhances noise robustness and generalizability. Extensive experiments on real-world datasets (Chikusei, Botswana, and EMIT) demonstrate remarkable inpainting quality over existing self-supervised and diffusion-based algorithms in both noiseless and noisy cases.

Conclusion: HyDiff-EI offers a practical and effective solution for HSI inpainting that avoids the need for large pretrained models, making it well-suited for real-world remote sensing applications with limited annotated data.

Abstract: A novel Hyperspectral diffusion Equivariant Imaging (HyDiff-EI) framework for solving the hyperspectral image (HSI) inpainting problem has been presented here. Unlike conventional diffusion-based methods that rely on large-scale pretraining, HyDiff-EI is a test-time optimization framework that learns directly from a single corrupted HSI acquisition. This makes it flexible for different sensor configurations and particularly well-suited for practical remote sensing scenarios where large annotated hyperspectral datasets are limited. To address the ill-posed nature of unsupervised inpainting, we embed equivariant consistency constraints within the diffusion process. By leveraging the inherent geometric symmetries and intrinsic characteristics of HSIs, HyDiff-EI bridges the gap between generative diffusion modeling and self-consistent physical priors. We empirically show that coupling diffusion modeling with equivariant priors substantially enhances noise robustness and generalizability. Extensive experiments on real-world datasets including Chikusei, Botswana, and EMIT demonstrate that HyDiff-EI offers remarkable inpainting quality over existing self-supervised and diffusion-based algorithms in both noiseless and noisy cases.

</details>


### [44] [LLaVAFlow: Preserving Latent Alignment Flow for Parameter-Efficient Multimodal Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26820)
*Muyao Yuan,Muyan Jiao,Jiangyong Ying,Weizhan Zhang,Yuanhong Zhang,Lan Ma,Yuan Gao,Haipeng Du*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: LLaVAFlow is an information-theoretic distillation framework that preserves cross-modal alignment in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) during visual instruction tuning, preventing catastrophic forgetting while enhancing downstream performance and generalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Visual instruction tuning for downstream tasks in MLLMs causes catastrophic forgetting, impairing overall generalization. Existing methods regulate weight updates but overlook the fundamental cross-modal alignment in MLLMs.

Method: LLaVAFlow uses a two-stage information-theoretic approach: (1) compress mutual information between extracted relations and MLLM embeddings to produce refined alignment flow benefiting downstream tasks; (2) maximize mutual information between extracted alignment flows of pretrained and fine-tuned MLLMs to transfer compact alignment information.

Result: Extensive experiments show LLaVAFlow is an effective plug-and-play framework that preserves alignment flow and enhances both downstream performance and generalization.

Conclusion: The information-theoretic distillation framework successfully addresses catastrophic forgetting in MLLMs by preserving cross-modal alignment through the information-compression trajectory, offering a generalizable solution for visual instruction tuning.

Abstract: While Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) exhibit strong generalization, visual instruction tuning for downstream tasks inevitably causes catastrophic forgetting, impairing overall generalization. While existing methods regulate weight updates to reduce forgetting, they overlook the fundamental cross-modal alignment in MLLMs. Based on prior work and our observations, we argue that cross-modal alignment is implicitly captured in the information-compression trajectory. To preserve the alignment flow embedded in the trajectory, we propose LLaVAFlow, an information-theoretic distillation framework. First, we compress the mutual information between the extracted relations and MLLM embeddings, encouraging a learnable module to produce a refined alignment flow that benefits downstream tasks. Second, we maximize the mutual information between the extracted alignment flows of the pretrained and fine-tuned MLLMs, enabling the transfer of compact alignment information. Extensive experiments show that LLaVAFlow is an effective plug-and-play framework that preserves alignment flow and enhances both downstream performance and generalization.

</details>


### [45] [Evaluator-Dependent Patient-Adaptive ECG Lead-Channel Allocation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26827)
*Xiaoyang Li,Zeyan Tao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that ECG channel acquisition policies trained with one diagnostic evaluator fail to transfer when the evaluator is replaced. In experiments on PTB-XL dataset, adaptive policies (ECG-on-Demand and MGA) trained with a logistic evaluator shifted from outperforming to underperforming fixed protocols when re-evaluated with a stronger ResNet1D evaluator. All 30 interaction estimates across different budgets and metrics were positive, indicating consistent evaluator dependence. The study concludes that adaptive ECG channel allocation should be developed jointly with its intended diagnostic backbone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The research addresses a fundamental problem in adaptive ECG acquisition: the value of acquiring any channel is defined relative to a downstream diagnostic evaluator, so marginal utilities learned under one evaluator may not transfer when the evaluator changes. This has practical implications for clinical deployment where the diagnostic backbone may differ from training.

Method: The authors froze two policies (ECG-on-Demand and MGA) trained with a controlled arbitrary-mask logistic evaluator, then scored their acquisition trajectories with a more predictive masked raw-waveform ResNet1D. They used exhaustive search to provide metric-matched population-wide fixed comparators separately for each evaluator. Three post-hoc sensitivity analyses were conducted: common-reference scoring, training the strong evaluator on policy-generated and random masks mixture, and evaluator-aligned Strong-MGA policy training.

Result: At budget k=4 on a held-out evaluation fold, ECG-on-Demand shifted from D_C=-0.011 (favoring adaptive under controlled evaluator) to D_S=+0.029 (favoring fixed under strong evaluator), yielding an NLL interaction of +0.041 (95% CI [+0.030, +0.050]). Across two policies, five budgets, and three probabilistic metrics, all 30 interaction estimates were positive with paired CIs excluding zero. Evaluator-aligned training reduced but did not eliminate the gap.

Conclusion: Adaptive ECG channel allocation should be developed and validated jointly with its intended diagnostic backbone. Jointly optimized sensing-diagnosis systems remain an open problem. Reference-choice and mask-distribution artifacts were ruled out as explanations through sensitivity analyses.

Abstract: Patient-conditioned acquisition policies for ECG lead-channel selection can outperform population-wide fixed protocols by tailoring the channel budget to each patient's observed cardiac state. However, the value of acquiring any given channel is defined relative to a downstream diagnostic evaluator, so marginal utilities learned under one evaluator need not transfer when the evaluator is replaced. We study this evaluator dependence empirically on PTB-XL by freezing two policies (ECG-on-Demand and MGA) trained with a controlled arbitrary-mask logistic evaluator, then scoring their unchanged acquisition trajectories with a more predictive masked raw-waveform ResNet1D. Exhaustive search provides metric-matched population-wide fixed comparators separately for each evaluator, enabling a clean interaction contrast. At budget $k=4$ on a held-out evaluation fold, ECG-on-Demand shifts from $D_\mathrm{C}=-0.011$ (favoring adaptive under the controlled evaluator) to $D_\mathrm{S}=+0.029$ (favoring fixed under the strong evaluator), yielding an NLL interaction of $+0.041$ (95% CI $[+0.030, +0.050]$). Across two policies, five budgets, and three probabilistic metrics, all 30 interaction estimates are positive with paired confidence intervals excluding zero. Three post-hoc sensitivity analyses -- common-reference scoring, training the strong evaluator on a mixture of policy-generated and random masks, and evaluator-aligned Strong-MGA policy training -- each preserve a positive interaction interval, making reference-choice and mask-distribution artifacts less plausible explanations. Evaluator-aligned training reduces but does not eliminate the gap. These results indicate that adaptive ECG channel allocation should be developed and validated jointly with its intended diagnostic backbone, and that jointly optimized sensing-diagnosis systems remain an open problem.

</details>


### [46] [Rethinking Image Processing for the Age of AI: A Problem-First Framework for Scientific Progress](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26833)
*Guoping Qiu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper critiques the prevalent "model-first" approach in AI-based image processing research, where researchers start with available architectures and optimize on benchmarks rather than addressing real-world imaging problems. The authors advocate for a "problem-first" approach through a six-stage workflow and argue for clearer research standards.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors observe that modern AI has expanded image processing capabilities, but the availability of powerful models, public datasets, and benchmark leaderboards has encouraged a model-first research pattern. This produces impressive benchmark results without necessarily improving solutions to real-world problems. The paper aims to address this mismatch between benchmark-defined tasks and actual real-world imaging problems.

Method: The authors employ case studies of super-resolution and low-light enhancement to demonstrate how benchmark datasets may define tasks substantially different from real-world problems. They propose a six-stage workflow: problem formulation, image acquisition, information-loss analysis, assumptions, ambiguity, and evaluation before model and dataset selection.

Result: The paper identifies that benchmark datasets often define tasks that differ substantially from the real-world problems they intend to represent. Performance improvements must be interpreted within the conditions under which they are obtained. The six-stage workflow provides a framework for placing problem understanding before model selection.

Conclusion: The paper calls for a change in research culture and education so that future researchers learn to understand imaging problems deeply and use modern AI to achieve genuine scientific and technical advancement. It proposes clearer standards for evidence, reproducibility, uncertainty, and claims of state-of-the-art performance.

Abstract: Modern AI has greatly expanded the capabilities of image processing. However, the ready availability of powerful models, public datasets, and benchmark leaderboards has also en- couraged a model-first research pattern: researchers increasingly begin with an available architecture and optimize it on a public benchmark, rather than beginning with the underlying real-world imaging problem. This can produce impressive benchmark results without necessarily improving our understanding or solution of the real problem. This paper argues for a problem-first approach that distinguishes the physical imaging problem, solution principle, statistical estimator, and computational implementation, while clarifying what modern AI can achieve and which fundamental problems remain unsolved. Through case studies of super- resolution and low-light enhancement, we show how benchmark datasets may define tasks that differ substantially from the real-world problems they are intended to represent, and why performance improvements must be interpreted within the conditions under which they are obtained. We propose a six-stage workflow that places problem formulation, image acquisition, information-loss analysis, assumptions, ambiguity, and evaluation before model and dataset selection. The paper also proposes clearer standards for evidence, reproducibility, uncertainty, and claims of state-of-the-art performance. More fundamentally, it calls for a change in research culture and education so that future researchers learn to understand imaging problems deeply and use modern AI to achieve genuine scientific and technical advancement.

</details>


### [47] [MedFG-VQA: Low-Frequency Memory and Graph Attention for Lightweight Medical VQA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26848)
*Haowen Gu,Gensheng Pei,Zeren Sun,Mingwu Ren,Xiangbo Shu,Yazhou Yao,Fumin Shen*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MedFG-VQA is a lightweight medical visual question answering framework that uses frequency-memory fusion and graph-aware cross-attention to achieve competitive performance with lower computational costs compared to larger models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Medical VQA faces challenges from limited annotated data and high computational demands of existing large vision-language models, hindering clinical deployment.

Method: Two key innovations: (1) Frequency-Memory Fusion (FMF) augments DCT-based low-frequency features using a learnable memory bank; (2) Graph-Aware Cross-Attention (GACA) aligns visual-textual features via cross-attention and refines them through graph-convolutional aggregation. Also constructs SynMed-VQA, a large-scale synthetic dataset with 2M+ QA pairs across 9 imaging modalities and 10 organs using GPT-4o.

Result: MedFG-VQA achieves competitive or superior performance on SynMed-VQA and three other standard biomedical VQA benchmarks while maintaining significantly lower computational costs compared to much larger models.

Conclusion: The framework demonstrates high efficiency and strong potential for clinical deployment due to its lightweight architecture and effective data augmentation approach.

Abstract: Medical Visual Question Answering (Med-VQA) holds significant promise for clinical decision support, yet faces challenges due to limited annotated data and the high computational demands of existing large vision-language models. We propose MedFG-VQA, a lightweight framework that leverages a memory bank to augment DCT-based low-frequency features and employs graph-enhanced cross-attention for effective visual-textual alignment. Specifically, our approach features two key components: Frequency-Memory Fusion (FMF), which enhances low-frequency features by retrieving from a learnable memory bank built on DCT decomposition, and Graph-Aware Cross-Attention (GACA), which aligns visual-textual features via cross-attention and refines them through graph-convolutional aggregation. To address data scarcity, we construct SynMed-VQA, a large-scale synthetic dataset comprising over 2 million question-answer pairs across 9 imaging modalities and 10 major organs, generated with GPT-4o. Extensive experiments on SynMed-VQA and three other standard biomedical VQA benchmarks demonstrate that MedFG-VQA achieves competitive or superior performance compared to much larger models while maintaining significantly lower computational costs, highlighting its efficiency and potential for clinical deployment.

</details>


### [48] [From Reasoning to Pixels: Grounded Medical Multimodal LLMs for VQA and Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.26856)
*Haowen Gu,Gensheng Pei,Junzhu Mao,Qiong Wang,Mingwu Ren,Yazhou Yao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MedREAL is a unified framework for Medical Visual Question Answering that combines linguistic reasoning with spatial grounding to address the limitation of current MLLMs that rely on global image features lacking precise pixel-level grounding. The framework uses SARP to extract task-relevant semantic evidence from [SEG] tokens and R2V fusion to inject reasoning-aware features into segmentation pipelines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) in Med-VQA rely on global image features, which lacks precise pixel-level grounding and limits clinical trustworthiness. There is a semantic gap between high-level clinical reasoning and spatial localization that needs to be bridged.

Method: The paper proposes MedREAL framework with two key components: 1) Seg Anchored Reasoning Pooling (SARP) - distills task-relevant semantic evidence from [SEG] tokens within MLLM's hidden states; 2) Reasoning-to-Visual (R2V) fusion mechanism - injects reasoning-aware features into segmentation pipeline for accurate mask decoding. Additionally, the authors construct MedRAVS-13K dataset with 13,824 expertly validated samples across four imaging modalities.

Result: MedREAL significantly outperforms state-of-the-art methods, achieving 68.49% gIoU and 70.47% cIoU on benchmark evaluations.
